# Ruling log

Status: planning state M0. This document is the ruling log according to [ADR 0001](adr/0001-adrs-in-madr-format-decisions-as-ruling-log.md): decisions with
reason and location that do not have their own ADR or that an ADR only summarizes. Load-bearing
decisions are ADRs in the [ADR index](adr/README.md). Every chapter of the
specification lists its own decisions with numbers such as `R-AR-01` in the section
"Decisions of this chapter"; the source is there, the reference is here. Decisions that
concern only a particular installation (its accounts, names, hosts, repository paths) are
not here but in the private operations repository of that instance ([ADR 0002](adr/0002-two-repos-public-product-private-ops-repo.md)).

## Rules for this log

1. **IDs are stable.** An ID is never reassigned, never renumbered and never deleted. An
   outdated row stays in place and names "superseded by" or "Current state:" in the decision, with the
   new ID or location.
2. **Every row has a decision, a reason and a location.** The location is a link to file and section.
   `make linkcheck` checks files and anchors; a renamed section breaks the gate.
3. **The chapter wins.** If a row deviates from the chapter, the chapter applies (specification first,
   [PLAN 1](PLAN.md#1-principles) principle 3), and the row is adjusted in the same change.
4. **New entries.** Build tickets record their rulings in section 10. A change to the
   specification needs an ADR or an entry here.
5. **Owner questions** are listed with their default in `OPEN-QUESTIONS.md`, product risks in
   `RISKS.md`; this log names only the decision that the plan uses until the answer.

## Reading guide: IDs

| Prefix | Meaning | Section |
|---|---|---|
| `D-B1` to `D-B13` | Fundamental decisions of the lead; in PLAN and chapters short `B1` to `B13` | 1 |
| `D-I1` to `D-I6` | Invariants of the backend policy and the terminal; short `I1` to `I6` | 2 |
| `D-R-<topic>` | Rulings of the lead from the research | 3 |
| `D-L1` to `D-L24`, `D-LS-<n>` | Rulings of the lead on deviations of the teams and further rulings without an ADR; short `L1` to `L24` | 4 |
| `D-<abbrev>-<n>` | Rulings of the planning teams, by chapter; the number counts the rulings of the chapter in the order of the team report and says nothing about the R-ID | 5 |
| `D-K-<Team><n>`, `D-K-X<n>`, `D-KB-<n>` | Core contract: requests from the teams, alignment of all documents, open items of the core report | 6 |
| `D-T1` to `D-T11` | Rulings of the lead on the addendum of 2026-10-10; short `T1` to `T11`. The later rulings of the lead `T12` (one session user per person), `T13` (no shared locations) and `T14` (preview images `terminal-*` and `viz-*`) are D-W-57, D-W-62 and D-W-77 in section 9; they are not `D-T12` to `D-T14` | 7, 9 |
| `D-T12` onward, `D-V<n>`, `D-N<n>` | Rulings of the writers of the addendum; the column "Writer ID" names their original number | 7 |
| `D-G-<n>` | Rulings of the gap integration that are in no chapter; `<n>` is the number of the gap item | 8 |
| `D-W-<n>` | Ruling log of the resumption | 9 |
| `R-<abbrev>-<n>` | Decision of a chapter; the abbreviation names the chapter: `PR` 01, `AR` 02, `DM` 03, `SY` 04, `AZ` 05, `API` 06, `RE` 07, `ID` 08, `AG` 09, `EX` 11, `FI` 12, `PN` 13, `SU` 14, `CL` 15, `OP` 16, `OB` 17, `RL` 18, `SE` 19, `DP` 20, `DV` 21, `IM` 22, `QA` 23, `TM` 24, `VZ` 25 | 8 |
| `P-<n>`, `W<n>` | Decisions and known contradictions of the plan ([PLAN 19](PLAN.md#19-decisions-of-this-plan), [PLAN 7](PLAN.md#7-m1-foundation-toolchain-and-server-core-with-permissions)) | 8 |

Chapter references name the number of the chapter and of the section, for example "02 3.3" for
[02 3.3](spec/02-architecture.md#33-commands-of-the-binary). The link leads to the section.

Sections 1 to 9 (the planning state and the resumption: IDs `D-B`, `D-I`, `D-R`, `D-L`, `D-LS`,
`D-<abbrev>`, `D-K`, `D-T`, `D-V`, `D-N`, `D-G` and `D-W`) stand in
[DECISIONS-PLANNING.md](DECISIONS-PLANNING.md), with the same IDs and numbering, so that this log stays below
`repo.max_file_kb`; section 10 and the sections of the build tickets stay here.

## 10. Rulings of the build milestones

From M1 on, every ticket records here what it decides and no chapter specifies: ID
`D-<Ticket>-<n>` (for example `D-M1-17-1`), date, decision, reason, location. If a ticket changes
a row above, the row stays and names the new ID.

| ID | Date | Decision | Reason | Location |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D-DEPS-1 | 2026-10-11 | The development tools pin `katex` through `overrides` in `tools/node/package.json` to `0.18.10`, the newest 0.18 release that is not deprecated (0.18.11 is deprecated by its authors: https://registry.npmjs.org/katex/0.18.11, retrieved 2026-10-11). The pin goes when mermaid and mermaid-cli accept a fixed katex themselves; Renovate proposes newer pins in the group "dev tools (Node)". | GHSA-238p-pmpm-9mq7 is fixed from 0.18.2 (https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-238p-pmpm-9mq7, retrieved 2026-10-11), but mermaid-cli 11.17.0 asks for `katex@^0.16.25` and mermaid (also the newest 12.1.0) for `^0.16.47` (https://registry.npmjs.org/@mermaid-js/mermaid-cli/11.17.0, https://registry.npmjs.org/mermaid/12.1.0, retrieved 2026-10-11), so the Dependabot security update failed with "conflicting dependencies" and no tool release resolves to a fixed version. mermaid-cli uses the installed package only for the katex style sheet; mermaid's browser bundle carries its own katex code, which the override does not change, and the repository's diagrams contain no math. `make diagrams` renders every diagram with the pin. | [toolchain.md](quality/toolchain.md#gate-tools), [package.json](../tools/node/package.json) |
| D-DEPS-2 | 2026-10-11 | GHSA-86w9-cpqp-85rv (node-forge up to 1.4.0, RSA PKCS#1 v1.5 signature verification) is dismissed on GitHub as "vulnerable code not used" and stays an accepted OSV finding with an expiry date (D-QA-19). No override and no removal: no node-forge release has the fix (https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-86w9-cpqp-85rv, retrieved 2026-10-11), and the newest `@sonar/scan` (5.0.1) pins node-forge 1.4.0 exactly (https://registry.npmjs.org/@sonar/scan/5.0.1, retrieved 2026-10-11). | `@sonar/scan` imports node-forge only in `src/request.js` (source of 5.0.1: https://github.com/SonarSource/sonar-scanner-npm/blob/bfa5f4128eb69f902e09190f7ad1a41dc65b1912/src/request.ts, default truststore in https://github.com/SonarSource/sonar-scanner-npm/blob/bfa5f4128eb69f902e09190f7ad1a41dc65b1912/src/properties.ts, retrieved 2026-10-11), to read a PKCS#12 truststore when `sonar.scanner.truststorePath` is set or `<sonar.userHome>/ssl/truststore.p12` exists; it calls `util.decode64`, `asn1.fromDer`, `pkcs12.pkcs12FromAsn1`, `getBags` and `certificateToPem`, none of which verifies an RSA signature (PKCS#12 integrity is an HMAC), and the certificates go to Node's TLS stack, which does the verification. The repository configures no truststore, and even with one the path verifies no signature. A probe on 2026-10-11 ran exactly these calls on a truststore with an RSA certificate (exponent 3) while every RSA verify entry point threw: zero calls. Replacing the scanner would need Java or Docker for the scan, which the toolchain avoids on purpose. | [osv-scanner.toml](../tools/node/osv-scanner.toml), [toolchain.md](quality/toolchain.md#gate-tools) |
| D-DEPS-3 | 2026-10-13 | Pull requests of the dependency bots `dependabot[bot]` and `renovate[bot]` pass CI like fork pull requests. `tools/ci/pr_mode.py` (job `plan` of `ci.yml`) sets `private_checks=false` when the pull request author and the actor of the run are both one of these logins: the deny list then runs with the generic rules (`denylist (generic rules, fork)`), `sonar` is skipped, and the run shows the notice "Reduced checks". `make commitlint` gets the pull request author as `PR_AUTHOR` and exempts from the DCO sign-off only the commits whose author e-mail is exactly that bot's noreply address (`BOT_AUTHORS` in `tools/hooks/commit_msg.py`); Conventional Commits still apply to them and to the title. Before the merge the maintainer rewrites the body with their own `Signed-off-by`, which runs every check again with the secrets and signs the squash commit on `main`. | GitHub gives a run that Dependabot starts no Actions secrets and treats it like a fork run (https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/dependabot/troubleshooting-dependabot/troubleshooting-dependabot-on-github-actions, retrieved 2026-10-13), so every Dependabot pull request failed on the missing private list, the missing SonarQube token and the missing sign-off (the bot signs with an address other than its author address). The rule depends on the pull request author, which only GitHub sets, and on the actor, which decides about the secrets: a bot address written into a commit of a fork or of a person's pull request exempts nothing, and a person's run on a bot pull request keeps every check and fails closed without a secret. Renovate gets the same rule so both bots follow one path. Dependabot takes the `build(deps)` prefix from the history of `main`; a title it writes outside the rules fails `commitlint`, and the maintainer fixes the title instead of the gate relaxing. | [push-discipline.md](quality/push-discipline.md#dependency-bot-pull-requests), [gates.md](quality/gates.md#skipping-and-failing), [ci.yml](../.github/workflows/ci.yml), [pr_mode.py](../tools/ci/pr_mode.py) |

### M1-01 Server gates

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-01/1 | 2026-10-10 | `<module>/migrations.sha256` holds the immutability of merged migrations; only `make migrations-lock` writes new lines, and it never changes an existing line. By hand, a branch removes only its own line that is not yet merged, when it renumbers. | ADR 0007 rule 5 and "Confirmation"; before the first push there is no diff against a remote base branch. |
| M1-01/2 | 2026-10-10 | The server gates require go, a Docker-compatible runtime and the pins as soon as a Go module is tracked, including gates without tests. | R-QA-02 and PLAN P-09: A host that cannot run the integration tests never reports a half-green server state. |
| M1-01/3 | 2026-10-10 | `codegen-check` detects drift through snapshots before and after the generator instead of through `git diff`. | Manual changes in a dirty working tree are not drift of the generator. |
| M1-01/4 | 2026-10-10 | `make perf` has the time limit `quality.perf.timeout`. | The default limit of `go test` would end long-running tests early; the value is a named threshold like all others in chapter 23. |
| M1-01/5 | 2026-10-10 | `tools/bin/tools.lock.json` is in the layout that `make tools-lock` writes (JSON with indentation 2); `tools/bin/tests/test_fetch_tools.py` holds that. | In the earlier compact layout, every re-pin rewrote the whole file, and the changed checksums got lost in the diff. |
| M1-01/6 | 2026-10-10 | `make migrations-lock` refuses on an unreadable line or a duplicate name in `migrations.sha256` and writes nothing; when it writes, it establishes the order and the line ending at the end of the file. | With an unreadable or duplicate line it would have to discard or choose a line, that is, change an existing line. |
| M1-01/7 | 2026-10-10 | `migrations-lint` skips a Go module only if neither `migrations/` nor `migrations.sha256` exists; a checksum file without a directory fails. | If `migrations/` is deleted while the checksum file stays, this is noticed. If both are deleted together, if a migration is changed by hand together with its line, or if the newest migration is removed together with its line, the gate does not see it; this limit is stated in `docs/quality/gates.md` under "What the gates do not cover". |
| M1-01/8 | 2026-10-10 | The recipes of the server gates explicitly abort with `exit 1` after every failed step instead of relying on `.SHELLFLAGS`. | GNU make 3.81 (macOS) does not know `.SHELLFLAGS` and would continue after an error. |
| M1-01/9 | 2026-10-10 | Go coverage is computed over a copy of `coverage.out` without the lines of the packages under `test/` and `perf/`; `coverage.out` itself stays unchanged. | SonarQube reads `coverage.out` in `make check` and in the CI job that only runs `make sonar`; only this way does it see the same file in both places. Until M1-72 adds `services/server/perf/**` to `sonar.test.inclusions` (brief M1-72, scope 8), SonarQube counts the files under `perf/` without `_test.go` (such as `perf/internal/report` from M1-16 on) as production code. |
| M1-01/10 | 2026-10-10 | `go_prereqs.py` reports every error while reading the lock file or while first loading a pin (network error, aborted download, broken lock file) as a problem of the gate (`<gate>: <tool>: <error>`). | An abort with a traceback does not say which tool is missing and loses the problems already collected. |
| M1-01/11 | 2026-10-10 | `tools/conftest.py` (new, outside the file list of the brief) removes, for every test, the variables with which Git hooks point to a repository (`GIT_DIR` and related ones); `codegen_check.py` does not pass them on to `git ls-files`. | The pre-push hook calls `make check`; from a linked worktree pytest inherits an absolute `GIT_DIR`, and the Git fixtures of the tests would have reconfigured the real repository and created a commit that deletes all files. The error class was already present in older Git fixtures under `tools/`; it is fixed in one place, and `tools/ci/tests/test_git_env_isolation.py` holds that. |

### M1-02 Action catalogue

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-02/1 | 2026-10-10 | The lists of known actions in `docs/api/redocly.yaml` (`authz_actions`, `receive_actions`) contain no system actions. | 05, test 7: no route and no op names a system action, and no event has one as its receive action; the lint enforces what a route test would otherwise only find later. |
| M1-02/2 | 2026-10-10 | The table "Scopes for apps" (05, 2.6) and the configuration values of 05 stay maintained by hand; `catalog-check` compares them with `docs/spec/actions.yaml`. | Their rows contain prose, and the configuration table feeds the configuration reference. |
| M1-02/3 | 2026-10-10 | `docs/spec/actions.yaml` carries no mapping of actions to modules. | The mapping belongs in the module register (ADR 0050, M1-06); one fact, one source. |
| M1-02/4 | 2026-10-10 | The list "Effect" (05, 2.7) stays prose. The check derives `effect` per action from it (suffixes `.read` and `.list`, explicitly named IDs, exceptions after "except") and compares; an unknown ID named there is a finding. | The list is a closed rule in words that sets new actions to `write` (fail closed); this way it stays readable, and a deviation between rule and file shows up in the gate. |
| M1-02/5 | 2026-10-10 | `channel_ops` follows all rows of the table "Terminal channels" in 05, including one `error` row each for the stream of the device and for the data connection of the host, which the summary in the brief did not name. | 05 is normative, and every row stays as it is (P-11); asyncapi.yaml has the same operations (`terminal.send.error`, `hostData.send.error`), and the schema expresses the rows without a change of text. |
| M1-02/6 | 2026-10-10 | `resource_version` is required for every event type, durable and ephemeral; the brief requires it only for durable ones, and requiring it for all is a deliberate tightening. It is `true` exactly when the table of the resource in 03 has a column `version` and the event reports its new value. It is `false` for the exceptions from 04 "Delivery guarantee" item 3, all resources without a column `version` (among others `user`, `file`, `instance`, `grant`, `group`, `principal`, `auth_session`, `gdpr_request`, `import_source`, `interaction`), `draft.deleted` and the ephemeral events except `run.updated`; `run.updated` is `true`. | Rule of the core contract: 04 "Envelope" gives all events the same envelope, and "Delivery guarantee" item 3 says when `resource.version` is set; without a required field, a silent default would decide. A draft is removed when it is deleted, so there is no new value; `settings.updated`, `notification_pref.updated` and `quiet_hours.updated` have the resource `user` without a column `version` and carry the version of their sub-object in the payload, like `read_state.updated`; `run.updated` reports the new `runs.version` (04 "Live box streaming"), the other ephemeral events report no new version of their object. |
| M1-02/7 | 2026-10-10 | `make catalog` writes nothing while `make catalog-check` reports a finding other than an outdated block content (invalid catalogue, contradiction with a hand-maintained part of 05, missing, unknown or broken markers, blocks in 05 out of the order of the catalogue or outside their section, deviations next to the markers); it only replaces outdated block contents. | No block is generated from an invalid catalogue, and what a generation cannot fix must be decided first. |
| M1-02/8 | 2026-10-10 | Beyond the rules of the brief, `catalog-check` checks: order of the actions by sections, durable before ephemeral event types, the receive cell starts with `self` exactly when `audience: self`, `action_text` names its actions, `also_actions` and `per_event_receive_action` only with `action_text`, cells without a line break, without an unescaped `\|` and without spaces at the edge, `right` names `system` only as exactly `system`, no duplicate keys in a YAML mapping. | Machine value and literal text must not drift apart, a broken cell would break the table in 05, `system ` would silently become a normal action, and PyYAML silently keeps the last of duplicate keys ([yaml/pyyaml#41](https://github.com/yaml/pyyaml/issues/41), retrieved 2026-10-11); every check has a test. |
| M1-02/9 | 2026-10-10 | The relational rules (uniqueness, sections, classes, events, ops) run only on a schema without findings. | On a broken schema they mostly just repeat its findings. |
| M1-02/10 | 2026-10-10 | `receivers` is in the file only for `agent` and `agent_or_app`; if it is missing for `audience: self`, `principal` applies. | The common case needs no line; the loader sets the default. |
| M1-02/11 | 2026-10-10 | `tools/catalog/tests` is in the `pythonpath` of pytest, like `sonarqube/tests`. | This way the test helper `table_import` can be imported without a package structure. |
| M1-02/12 | 2026-10-10 | ADR 0049 now also names the hand-maintained list "Effect" (2.7) and the attribute `resource_version` per event type (04 "Delivery guarantee"), and its section "More Information" refers to the row "machine-readable action catalogue" in 02, section 20, instead of quoting its old wording; otherwise it stays unchanged. | In the first two places the implementation deviates from the text of the ADR; this ticket reworded the row in 02, so the quote would otherwise be wrong. |
| M1-02/13 | 2026-10-11 | Next to the blocks, fail closed applies, and tests against the real artifacts hold the class. Static: chapter 05 starts with its `# ` heading, outside the blocks every heading has a visible title (a letter or a digit from an allow-list) made only of characters from an allow-list, the generated blocks appear in the order of the catalogue (in `redocly.yaml` the order of the rules is free, it does not change the lint), every table is in its level-2 section and every action table under the heading of its catalogue section, the static view reads headings by an approximation of the block rules of Python-Markdown (ATX only with `#` in the first column, Setext only under a line that starts a block; each block rule only on the form of a single line, so that among other things containers such as quotes, lists and definition lists, the interplay of several block rules in one block, block rules across lines and markup in the title are seen only by the render test), none of the characters that the check and Markdown read differently is present (line separators only for `str.splitlines`, STX and ETX, which Markdown removes), and outside the generated blocks there is no HTML, no heading without a space after `#`, no brace pair outside a code span (code spans and escapes per line like the docs site, that is like `find_code_spans` from pymdownx.inlinehilite in the version from `tools/uv.lock`; table rows and spans across several lines are seen only by the render test), no admonition, no collapsible block and no footnote, and in no section a line with `\|` or `<table`, not even in a code block, except in the hand-maintained sections, whose titles each occur exactly once. `docs/api/redocly.yaml` stays within an allow-list (at the top only `extends` with exactly `[recommended]`, `apis` and `rules`; per API only `root` and `rules`; roots relative and unique after resolution; no key with `$`; no duplicate keys), the lint rules of the catalogue keep their generated form, no rule set under `apis` names them, and `docs/api/.redocly.lint-ignore.yaml` names none. Against the artifacts: a test renders chapter 05 with the Markdown configuration from `mkdocs.yml` (no page metadata according to mkdocs, the same sequence of headings as the static view, only elements and attributes from an allow-list, no raw HTML except the markers according to the renderer, outside the hand-maintained sections exactly the generated tables, each in the section and under the heading of its block, no heading without a visible title, with a character outside the allow-list or in a list item or quote, and in 2.6, 2.7 and the configuration table exactly what the check reads there, which statically requires exactly one table each with a fixed header row and separator row or only list items with `- ` in the first column without a heading, no second heading with the same letters as the title of the section (without digits, `I` equal to `l`), and lets the section end like Markdown at every line with at most as many `#`); a test compares the static view of code spans and escapes line by line with the renderer; an end-to-end test sets every catalogue property of the contracts at every place to an excluded value and requires exactly as many errors from the real `make api-lint`; the recipes of `make api-lint`, `make catalog` and `make catalog-check` are pinned, a direct Redocly run checks the unchanged contracts without make, and `make catalog-check` must fail on a copy with an outdated block. | The red team and the review repeatedly found the same error class: the lint no longer works as generated, or a hand-written table escapes generation, and the gate stays green; every static list of forms left another form open. Following the rule on a recurring error class, tests against the real artifacts therefore hold the class; the static checks give fast feedback. What no self-test can cover (Makefile contents outside the pinned recipes in interaction with other files, Git state, CI, settings in `mkdocs.yml` other than `markdown_extensions`, deliberate changes to `tools/catalog` and its tests) is listed in tools/catalog/README.md and belongs to the review and the red team gate; M1-03 brings the direct comparison of the contracts. |
| M1-02/14 | 2026-10-11 | No test of `tools/catalog` measures wall-clock time. A test without a clock (a text that counts the characters read) holds that the line numbers of findings about foreign line separators come from a running counter; tests over the process time of the single scan hold that code spans and headings read long lines linearly, with a limit far above linear reading and far below the earlier quadratic or cubic reading of the same line. | The gates run on any machine and under load (PLAN P-07, `docs/quality/gates.md`); a wall-clock limit around a whole `check()` failed at random on efficiency cores and under load (Red Team M1-02, finding 1). Process time does not depend on the load, and the margin on both sides covers slower cores. |
| M1-02/15 | 2026-10-11 | A cell of a generated table must stay exactly one cell on the site; the cell rule of M1-02/8 ("without an unescaped `\|`") applies in this form. To that end, the rule `schema` mimics how the extension `tables` of Python-Markdown splits a row: `\\` is an escaped backslash, `\|` an escaped `\|`, a backslash before backticks escapes one of them, every backtick run pairs with the next later run of the same length, and only a `\|` outside a pair splits. A cell passes if every run that it opens closes in it and no `\|` is outside a pair; otherwise a finding line names entry, field and column. A test compares the rule with the renderer for all cells of up to `_MAX_BUILDING_BLOCKS` building blocks (a constant of the tests), a test renders every generated table and compares it cell by cell with the catalogue (`chapter_table_cells`). | Red Team M1-02, finding 2: an open backtick and `\\\|` broke the table in 05, and the gate stayed green because the static rule looked back only one character and knew no code spans. It is the same error class as in M1-02/13 (the static view deviates from the renderer); the comparison with the real renderer holds it, the static rule gives the fast finding line. A `\|` in a code span now passes, because the site shows it that way. |
| M1-02/16 | 2026-10-11 | The findings of `catalog-check` come in stages, and a stage runs only if the stages before it found nothing: reading and parsing, duplicate keys and recursive aliases, schema, relational rules (M1-02/9), then all rules on chapter 05 and `redocly.yaml` together. Every finding is one line, including a parse error, a value that Python cannot build (invalid date, number above the digit limit) and nesting that is too deep; the check reads aliases once per node. `make catalog` writes chapter 05 and `redocly.yaml` together or neither: a file that cannot be written stops it before the first write, and if a replacement fails, the file already replaced gets its old text back. | Red Team M1-02, findings 3 to 7: a recursive alias, an invalid date or a huge number aborted the check with a traceback, nested aliases grew exponentially, a parse error spanned four lines, `$` let an op name with a line break through, and a read-only `redocly.yaml` left 05 generated and `redocly.yaml` old. Stages, because later rules on a broken state mostly just repeat earlier findings; the README and docstrings name them so that new findings after a fix do not come as a surprise. |
| M1-02/17 | 2026-10-11 | ADR 0049, section "Confirmation", now names what `make catalog-check` reads (`actions.yaml`, chapter 05, `redocly.yaml`, the ignore file of Redocly); `make codegen-check` keeps the generated constants in `kernel/authorize` and the route table in `api/routes` current (M1-25, M1-17). The README and docstrings no longer name version numbers of dependencies, but the version from `tools/uv.lock`. | Red Team M1-02, findings 16 and 17: `check()` reads only these files, and a number in the docs that no test holds silently goes stale at the next update. |
| M1-02/18 | 2026-10-11 | Two titles look the same if they show the same letters, either without digits or read with `1` as `l` and `0` as `o` (addendum to M1-02/13). Between the heading under which a generated table stands and the table there is no heading of level 4 or deeper; checked statically and on the rendered page. | Red Team M1-02, findings 10 and 11: a copy of "Configuration values of this chapter" with `1` instead of `l` passed both layers, and a level-4 heading with the title of another section directly above an action table showed a reader a foreign title. Chapter 05 has no level-4 heading. |
| M1-02/19 | 2026-10-11 | The cell rule of the rule `schema` (M1-02/15) also rejects HTML (`<` before a letter, `!`, `?` or `/`) and brace pairs `{...}` outside the code spans of a cell, read like the lines of 05 outside the blocks; `make catalog-check` reports this in one line, and `make catalog` writes nothing. The sentence in `tools/catalog/README.md` that every cell stays exactly one cell on the site remains and names the two cases. | Red Team M1-02 (second round), Minor 1: raw HTML or an attribute list in a cell passed `catalog-check`, only the render tests in `make test` failed, and the README promised more for `catalog-check` than the rule checked. Rejecting instead of narrowing the sentence, because the rule fails closed: no text in `actions.yaml` needs HTML or a brace pair outside a code span, a path like `{session_id}` is in one and passes. The rule is stricter than the renderer (even `<b>`, which does not split the cell, is a finding); the cell-by-cell comparison with the renderer still holds the rest of the class. |
| M1-02/20 | 2026-10-11 | A profiling timer (`signal.setitimer` with `ITIMER_PROF`) enforces the process-time limit of the tests of `tools/catalog` (M1-02/14): it counts process time like `time.process_time` and stops the measured work at the limit with a test failure. | Red Team M1-02, Minor 2: without the memo list of the aliases, the two alias tests ran for minutes instead of turning red; a limit that measures only after the end reports a regression as a hanging run. Process time instead of wall-clock time, because the gates run under load (P-07); the test failure comes from the signal handler as `pytest.fail`, so that no `except Exception` in the checked code swallows it. `signal.setitimer` exists on Unix, so on Linux and macOS, in CI and under WSL (CONTRIBUTING; [Python `signal.setitimer`](https://docs.python.org/3/library/signal.html#signal.setitimer), "Availability: Unix", retrieved 2026-10-11). |
| M1-02/21 | 2026-10-11 | `make catalog` cleans up on every exception, not only on `OSError`: files already replaced get their old text back, no temporary file remains, and an exception other than `OSError` (for example an abort with Ctrl-C) reaches the caller unchanged, with a note per file whose old text did not come back. A write error is `CatalogWriteError`, a subclass of `CatalogError`; the last line then reads `cannot write the generated blocks; nothing written` or, if a file kept its new text, names these files, instead of reporting `catalogue or blocks invalid`. | Red Team M1-02, Minors 3 and 4: an abort between writing and replacing left a temporary file in `docs/spec`, and a write error with a valid catalogue reported an invalid catalogue. Write back on an abort too, because `make catalog` writes both files together or neither (M1-02/16); the subclass leaves callers that catch `CatalogError` (M1-03, M1-06) unchanged. |
| M1-02/22 | 2026-10-11 | If several headings look like the title of a read section (M1-02/18) but not all carry exactly this title, the finding names each with its line and says that they look the same; `appears <n> times` stays only for literally equal titles. | Red Team M1-02, Minor 6: `### 9.1 Scopes for apps 2` gave `section 'Scopes for apps' appears 2 times`, although the title appears literally only once. The rule stays; the finding now shows which headings are meant. |
| M1-02/23 | 2026-10-11 | Ported onto the English `main`, the catalogue reads chapter 05 in English: section titles, header rows, cell words (`yes`, `no`, `denials`, `conditional`, `fresh`, `as target action`), directions and the word `except` in 2.7 follow the translated chapter, and `docs/spec/actions.yaml` is derived again from the translated chapter with the ticket's own importer; every machine field (IDs, classes, sections, flags, receive actions, ops) is unchanged, only the verbatim cell texts are English. The frozen copy for the migration proof is the translated chapter before the markers. Title characters are ASCII letters and digits only. | All repository content is English (D-W-94); a catalogue that kept the German texts would generate German tables into an English chapter, and the language gate rejects German text. |

### M1-03 API contract checks

The rulings on `x-problem-codes` and `x-status`, on `x-idempotency-exempt` and on
`x-deprecated-at` are R-API-23 to R-API-25 in
[06-api.md](spec/06-api.md#19-decisions-of-this-chapter), section 19.

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-03/1 | 2026-10-11 | The close codes in `asyncapi.yaml` are compared with 04 "Close codes" only by code and name; `client_action` is not. | Ruling L5: the texts in `asyncapi.yaml` are written for the API, and the meaning stays in 04. |
| M1-03/2 | 2026-10-11 | Problem codes that the router sends without a documenting operation carry `router: true` in `x-problem-codes`; only they may stand in no response component. | Otherwise the check "every code is named by a response" would fail on `method_not_allowed`. |
| M1-03/3 | 2026-10-11 | `api.deprecation_min_notice` is read in the section "Named values of all chapters" of the configuration reference through the columns `Key` and `Default`, not through the first cell; a duration may carry any unit from section 2 of the configuration reference (`ms`, `s`, `m`, `h`, `d`), not only `s`, `m`, `h`, `d` as in the brief, with at most six digits. | M1-08 may add columns, also in front of `Key`; a valid value of the configuration reference must not break the gate. The configuration reference has many tables with these columns; the process configuration is in this section, and sections 6 and 7 are other sources. |
| M1-03/4 | 2026-10-11 | `make api-tables` writes nothing while an input is unreadable or malformed, a value cannot become a table cell (missing, no text, with `\|`, a line break or a backtick in a code span, an operation without a declared tag) or a marker is broken, unknown or missing. Differences found by the rule `api-catalogue-sync` do not stop the generation. | A table from a broken source would silently lose rows; the gate reports the differences between two valid sources independently of the table. |
| M1-03/5 | 2026-10-11 | Beyond the sub-checks of the brief, `api-catalogue-sync` checks: duplicate YAML keys, values that PyYAML cannot build, wrong or empty containers and path entries that point only through `$ref` to `components.pathItems`, as the finding `source`; `x-authz-action` as an object or a list; unresolvable references to channels and messages; exactly one operation per channel, op and direction also at the terminal channels; event messages also where they stand directly in a channel, and each only once; `x-receive-action` or `x-audience` without `x-event-kind`; `x-idempotency-exempt` (also with an empty value) at a path entry, another method or a webhook; the header `Idempotency-Key` case-insensitively and only as a header (also behind `$ref`); a closed list of keys per entry in `x-problem-codes` and texts without German umlauts or sharp s. Example values (`example`, `examples`) do not count for the deprecation, names under `properties` are no fields, and recursive YAML aliases do not loop. `api-tables` also reports missing `tags` and an operation with an unresolvable channel reference. Copies of catalogue values in the schemas are checks of their own: `AppScope` equal to the scopes of the catalogue (sub-check `app-scopes`), the union `Event` equal to the durable types, the `type` enum of every event schema equal to its type (also for the webhooks), the `op` enum of every frame equal to its op, and on the terminal channels a close code below 4100 only with its name from 04. | PyYAML keeps the last value of duplicate keys silently, the Redocly enumeration skips objects, an operation behind a `$ref` or in a channel would otherwise meet no check, and each of these gaps would let a wrong contract stay green or a row drop silently out of a table; a broken input gives findings, never a traceback: every sub-check and every block runs protected, and an unexpected error becomes its finding (`input not checkable`, `input not renderable`), because the same error class appeared a second time after the final review. Every check has a test. |
| M1-03/6 | 2026-10-11 | Differences found: `file_blocked` stood in no response component; `Conflict` now names it (source completed). `dispatch` in `asyncapi.yaml` did not follow the order of the catalogue (`host.updated` and `terminal_session.changed` after the ephemeral events, the ephemeral ones among themselves in another order); the list now follows it (source reordered, `asyncapi.yaml` 0.1.1). The block `idempotency_exempt` in 06 did not name the five host and terminal operations that `openapi.yaml` exempted with `auth_flow`: `pairHost` (redeems a code without a login), `openTerminalSession` (consumes a fresh step-up) and `attachTerminalSession` (makes older session tokens worthless) stay `auth_flow`, and the copy now names them; `createHost` and `issueHostPairingCode` now carry the parameter `IdempotencyKey` and name the response `422` (`idempotency_key_reuse`), and a replay answers `409 conflict` with `reason: one_time_secret` (source corrected, `openapi.yaml` 0.2.0). The enum `AppScope` did not name `costs:reconcile`, although the catalogue assigns it to the action `cost.reconcile.submit` (05 section 2.6, ADR 0055); it now contains it (copy completed). | Like 05, the table in 06 put the durable before the ephemeral events, but the last four ephemeral ones stood in another order than in the catalogue; now list and table follow the order of the catalogue and of 05 completely. The order of the list in `asyncapi.yaml` carries no meaning (PATCH). `createHost` and `issueHostPairingCode` hand out a pairing code once, logged in and with an audit entry (24, sections 3.2 and 3.3); for this, 06 section 7 explicitly names `Idempotency-Key` with `one_time_secret` ("POST with a one-time secret in the response", R-API-26), as for `createEnrollmentCode`, and the brief allows an exemption only where a reason clearly fits. By the table in 06 section 13, changing the idempotency rule of an op is a breaking change; it is acceptable here because `0.x` applies before the first publication, hence MINOR. |
| M1-03/7 | 2026-10-11 | No call of `api_checks.py` ends in a traceback, and one place closes the class: `main` turns every error that no protected part reports into a finding line `<rule>: source: input not checkable (...)` (for `generate`: `input not renderable`) with exit 1. The protected parts still give precise findings: every sub-check, every block and now also the form check of every API file (finding `source` of the file). `generate` writes every file it changes to a temporary file next to it first and replaces the targets only when all are complete; a file that cannot be opened for writing or written is a finding, and no file is changed. Only if the rename itself fails does the message name the files already replaced instead of "nothing written". A table cell is only what `str.splitlines` reads as one line, so nothing with U+2028 and related characters either. | The red team of the first round found the same error class a third time: a YAML key whose integer `str()` cannot print (5000 hex digits; Python limits the conversion of large integers to strings, [integer string conversion length limitation](https://docs.python.org/3/library/stdtypes.html#integer-string-conversion-length-limitation), retrieved 2026-10-12) ended in a traceback in the form check, as did a read-only target file in `generate` (if it was the second one, the first was already new), and a cell with U+2028 and marker text broke a marker in `generate`. Writing in place left a half-written file behind on a write error in the middle of the file (full disk, size limit); the final review of this round found that. By the rule on recurring error classes, one place now catches it, tested against the real files; the marker functions of `tools/catalog` split the text with `str.splitlines`, so the same line rule applies to the cells as there (M1-02). |
| M1-03/8 | 2026-10-11 | `contentType: application/octet-stream` exempts a frame from the check of the `op` enum only on a terminal channel and only if it names no `op`; at the gateway never. | The gateway knows only JSON frames (04 section 2.3); only the Noise frame of the terminal channels is binary. The earlier exemption let every frame marked as binary pass, also a gateway frame with a wrong `op` (regression, red team of the first round). |
| M1-03/9 | 2026-10-11 | A message that stands inline instead of through `$ref` in `messages` of an AsyncAPI operation (also with `$ref: null`) is a finding of the sub-check `ws-ops` or `channel-ops`, at the gateway also of the block `ws_ops` (`make api-tables` then writes nothing); it does not also run through the sub-check `events`. | AsyncAPI 3 requires `$ref` pointers to the messages of the channel there ([AsyncAPI 3.0.0, Operation Object](https://www.asyncapi.com/docs/reference/specification/v3.0.0#operationObject), retrieved 2026-10-12). A copy written inline in `dispatch` ran past `events` into the table (red team of the first round). The rule in one place, when reading the operations, covers the gateway and the terminal channels and both rules, and is cheaper than a second check of the copy. |
| M1-03/10 | 2026-10-12 | Ported onto the English `main`: the generated tables of 06 and `webhooks.md` are rendered in English, equal to main's translated blocks; the tool reads 04 "Close codes" and the configuration columns `Key` and `Default`; the check for German letters in problem texts builds its character class from code points; README, rulings and test data are English. The port's regenerated block `idempotency_exempt` also lists `renewInvocationToken` (`agent_runtime`), which `openapi.yaml` exempts and main's copy did not list. Main's `Conflict` already named `file_blocked` (at the end of its list) and main's `AppScope` already listed `costs:reconcile`; the port keeps one entry of `file_blocked`, in the place the ticket gave it, and leaves `AppScope` unchanged. | All repository content is English (D-W-94); the generated blocks must equal what main shows, and the gate G3 that this ticket brings found the missing row. |

### M1-04 Operations configurations in the gate

| No. | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-04/1 | 2026-10-11 | The validators run as pinned release binaries through `tools/bin/fetch_tools.py`, not as container images. | The same way as every other gate tool, no Docker for a pure docs change, a checksum per platform. |
| M1-04/2 | 2026-10-11 | `fetch_tools.py` reads zip archives (recognized by the signature `PK\x03\x04`; the member must exist and be a regular file). | Loki publishes only zip assets (besides rpm and deb packages) for its binaries ([Loki v3.7.8 release](https://github.com/grafana/loki/releases/tag/v3.7.8), retrieved 2026-10-12). |
| M1-04/3 | 2026-10-11 | Pairs of collector overlays are checked in both orders; `none` stands alone in the check only. | Overlays replace lists when merging (by default the value of the last configuration source is used, [confmap README](https://github.com/open-telemetry/opentelemetry-collector/blob/eb01b88ec3bf496bf554f6b78d7382aa07a7de81/confmap/README.md#L171), retrieved 2026-10-12), so the result depends on the order of `BOTHY_OTEL_EXPORTER` and `BOTHY_OTEL_EXPORTER_2`; `none` is empty and changes no pair. |
| M1-04/4 | 2026-10-11 | The pins of validators whose component also runs as an image (`otelcol-contrib`, `promtool`, `amtool`, `loki`, `tempo`) follow the image versions: Renovate raises the images (group "Compose-Images", ADR 0041), is off for these validators, and the check `tool-versions` keeps both equal. | A newer or older validator accepts other keys or rejects others than the running component; the images need an update path, and one pull request per raise keeps image and validator together. |
| M1-04/5 | 2026-10-11 | The routing of Alertmanager is part of the gate (`amtool config routes test` for `page`, `ticket` and `Watchdog`). | alerting.md named the routes as checked by hand; a wrong route silences page alerts. |
| M1-04/6 | 2026-10-11 | The check `collector` sets the host name `validate.example.com` in its working copy of `exporters/datadog.yaml`; the overlay in the repository stays unchanged. | Otherwise `validate` queries metadata endpoints of clouds (network) and waits about 25 seconds: without `hostname` the exporter determines the host name automatically and waits up to `hostname_detection_timeout`, default `25s` ([example configuration of the exporter](https://github.com/open-telemetry/opentelemetry-collector-contrib/blob/7770a2a2da5a867e6f39cde9843927ca8b1a5353/exporter/datadogexporter/examples/collector.yaml#L166-L174), lines 166 to 174 and 451 to 455, retrieved 2026-10-12); operation keeps the automatic host name detection. The exporter queries the GCP metadata even with a fixed host name; M1-04/22 stops that. |
| M1-04/7 | 2026-10-11 | Every validator runs with exactly `PATH`, `HOME` and `TMPDIR` in the working folder of its check, `PATH` is an empty folder, plus `HTTP_PROXY` and `HTTPS_PROXY` (also in lower case) on a closed port of `127.0.0.1` (`NO_NETWORK_PROXY`) and only the variables the check names. | Neither the shell of the developer nor a program from her search path can change the result; all seven validators run without other programs (checked locally on 2026-10-11). The proxy stops HTTP downloads that use it: the final review found that `tofu init` downloads module sources from a registry from the network despite the empty provider mirror; with the proxy, such a download fails at once and becomes a finding. Clients with their own transport bypass it; M1-04/22 covers that. |
| M1-04/8 | 2026-10-11 | The validators always come from the tool chain of the checkout in which `opscheck.py` lies (`tools/bin/tools.lock.json`, cache `.cache/tools/`), regardless of `--root`; the check `tool-versions`, however, reads the lock file under `--root`. | `--root` names the checked files, not the tool chain; the tests check copies under `tmp_path` with the real pins without downloading them again. |
| M1-04/9 | 2026-10-11 | The check `tofu` gives `tofu` an empty local mirror as its only provider source (`provider_installation { filesystem_mirror }`) instead of an empty CLI configuration ([OpenTofu CLI configuration, provider installation](https://opentofu.org/docs/cli/config/config-file/), retrieved 2026-10-12). | With an empty configuration, `tofu init` would have downloaded providers from the registry, a network call the brief forbids; this way a module with providers fails, as the brief requires for `ops-check`, until a pinned way without network is defined. The proxy from M1-04/7 stops module sources from a registry or HTTP, the empty `PATH` stops Git sources. |
| M1-04/10 | 2026-10-11 | `BothyMalwareScanFailing` reads `... > 0 unless sum(rate(bothy_file_scan_total{bothy_outcome=~"clean\|infected"}[15m])) > 0` instead of `... > 0 and sum(...) == 0`. | The new unit test showed: without a series `clean` or `infected` (the scanner has never worked since the start), the sum is empty instead of 0, and `and` suppressed the alert exactly in this case (`vector1 and vector2` keeps only the elements of `vector1` with matching elements in `vector2`, [PromQL logical operators](https://prometheus.io/docs/prometheus/latest/querying/operators/#logical-set-binary-operators), retrieved 2026-10-12). |
| M1-04/11 | 2026-10-11 | The firing cases of the alert unit tests lie just above the threshold (a small step past it) and check the alert at the first evaluation at which it may fire: in the minute its `for` runs out or, for an alert without `for`, in the first minute its condition holds, with a quiet case one minute earlier (Watchdog fires from the first evaluation on and has no earlier case). Corrected by M1-04-minors: before, the claim "quiet one minute earlier" also stood for the alerts without `for`, whose firing cases came one minute late. | Locally on 2026-10-11: every threshold of `bothy-alerts.yaml` moved one percent up or down (for threshold 0 to 1 and -0.5) and every `for` removed, made one minute longer or shorter broke at least one case, 71 of 71 mutations caught. The red team on d73c418 found that an added `for: 1m` on the five alerts without `for` passed every case. `test_alert_unit_tests_hold_every_for_duration` now adds a `for` to every alert without one and makes every other `for` one minute longer and shorter (removes it at one minute), and expects a broken case of that alert for each. |
| M1-04/12 | 2026-10-11 | `BOTHY_TAILSCALE_VERSION` in `infra/compose/.env.example` carries the Renovate comment too; the group "Compose-Images" selects by files, not by manager, and switches off the digest pins for these two files. | The regex manager for `compose.yaml` reads every default `${BOTHY_<X>_VERSION:-...}`, so also the one of Tailscale (package rules select by `matchFileNames`, [Renovate configuration options](https://docs.renovatebot.com/configuration-options/#packagerulesmatchfilenames), retrieved 2026-10-12); without the comment, an update would have changed only one of the two files. The variables carry the version tags that operators read and set; a digest pin could not be written without `currentDigest` in the pattern. |
| M1-04/13 | 2026-10-11 | `markdown_table` finds a heading by its title without `#`, without a section number and without a parenthesis at the end ("Further alerts" for "3.2 Further alerts (`bothy-alerts.yaml`)"); headings and tables in code blocks do not count. | A new numbering of the sections does not break the checks; an example in a code block is never read as a source. |
| M1-04/14 | 2026-10-11 | `tools/opscheck/tests/test_opscheck.py` loads `opscheck.py` explicitly from its file. | The import mode `importlib` of pytest ([pytest import modes](https://docs.pytest.org/en/stable/explanation/pythonpath.html#import-modes), retrieved 2026-10-12) names the test module `opscheck.tests.test_opscheck` and creates the folder `tools/opscheck` as the package `opscheck`; a plain `import opscheck` would find this empty package instead of the tool. The brief prescribes path and name of the tool. |
| M1-04/15 | 2026-10-11 | `infra/README.md`, `infra/tofu/README.md`, the ADRs 0012, 0034, 0035 and 0058 and `docs/observability/dashboards.md` stay unchanged, although they name checks that `ops-check` now runs. | The brief allows changes only to the named files; these places describe earlier manual checks or the confirmation of an ADR and are still correct. |
| M1-04/16 | 2026-10-11 | `slo-docs` writes the target with one decimal and with more only where one decimal would round the target (`99.95 %`). | The rule of the brief ("one decimal") would have required the wrong row `100.0 %` for a target such as 99.95 and missed a difference in the second decimal; for all current targets the result is the same. |
| M1-04/17 | 2026-10-11 | `prometheus-config` also requires: every entry of `rule_files` lies under `/etc/prometheus/` (a relative entry counts, as in Prometheus, from the folder of `prometheus.yaml`) and hits at least one file, and every file in `rules/` is loaded. | `rule_files` takes file globs ([Prometheus configuration](https://prometheus.io/docs/prometheus/latest/configuration/configuration/), retrieved 2026-10-12), and `promtool check config` accepts a pattern without hits (measured with the pinned promtool, the test of this rule holds it); a typo in the pattern would have let Prometheus start without a single rule, page alerts included, and the gate would have stayed green. |
| M1-04/18 | 2026-10-11 | `alert-thresholds` reads the right-hand side of the last `>` comparison without PromQL comments and strings up to `)`, `and`, `or`, `unless` or the end; if it is not a plain number, that is a finding. | A comment (`# before > 1`), a `>` in a label value or arithmetic (`> 93600 * 2`) would otherwise have given a wrong number, in the last case even a false success. |
| M1-04/19 | 2026-10-11 | `tofu` copies `infra/tofu/` once as a whole and checks every module in this copy. | A module with a sibling as its source (`../common`) would otherwise have failed depending on the alphabetical order of the names. |
| M1-04/20 | 2026-10-11 | Code blocks in Markdown end, as in CommonMark, only with a line of the same character in at least the same length, also indented in a list ([CommonMark 0.31.2, fenced code blocks](https://spec.commonmark.org/0.31.2/#fenced-code-blocks), retrieved 2026-10-12); `tool-versions` reads `.env.example` as Compose does (quotation marks and a comment after a space and `#` do not count, also together; [Compose .env file syntax](https://docs.docker.com/compose/how-tos/environment-variables/variable-interpolation/#env-file-syntax), retrieved 2026-10-12); `--only` may appear several times; an empty `exporters/` or `slo/` is a finding. | Findings of the final review: a mixed code block, a quoted value or a repeated `--only` would have given wrong results, and an empty directory would have checked nothing and still reported `ok`. |
| M1-04/21 | 2026-10-11 | The header text of `renovate.json` names the exception of the Compose images from the digest pins. | The group "Compose-Images" switches them off (M1-04/12); otherwise the header text promised digest pins for all images. |
| M1-04/22 | 2026-10-11 | Every validator gets `GCE_METADATA_HOST` on the same closed port of `127.0.0.1` as the proxy. `tools/opscheck/tests/test_opscheck.py::test_no_validator_reaches_the_network` runs all checks with the real pins in a sandbox that ends every validator on a connection beyond loopback; the test runs only on macOS (`sandbox-exec`) and is skipped otherwise. | The red team found connections on port 80 to a fixed address in every run with the Datadog overlay, past the proxy. Narrowed down locally: only the Datadog exporter (not the connector) probes, also with a fixed host name and `host_metadata.enabled: false`; `AWS_EC2_METADATA_DISABLED` changes nothing, `GCE_METADATA_HOST` ends it (before, 30 of 30 runs blocked, after, 0 of 90). The GCP metadata library has its own transport without a proxy (its default client builds an `http.Transport` without a `Proxy` function, [metadata.go](https://github.com/googleapis/google-cloud-go/blob/f06f6b35e4b7ae8b48a3f9d1715229fe81cb2ebd/compute/metadata/metadata.go#L71-L86), retrieved 2026-10-12). On Linux there is no lock that makes an attempt visible (`unshare -n` only prevents it silently); `docs/quality/gates.md` states this limit. |
| M1-04/23 | 2026-10-11 | If a cell of the column "Configuration value" in alerting.md names a key of the configuration reference, it must read exactly `` `<name>` `` or ``above `<name>` `` (a no-break space after "above" counts as a space); any other form is a finding. | Finding of the red team: a reformatted cell (`` `backup.max_age` (26 h) ``, a key without backticks) would have silently dissolved the coupling of threshold and value. |
| M1-04/24 | 2026-10-11 | An alert counts as tested in `prometheus-rule-tests` only when a case sees it fire (non-empty `exp_alerts`). | Finding of the red team: an alert with only silent cases counted as tested, although no case shows that it fires at all. |
| M1-04/25 | 2026-10-11 | `collector-allowlist` also checks that the allowlist works: `allow_all_keys` is false, every trace and log pipeline (base and overlays) with a receiver that is no connector runs `redaction/allowlist`, and no overlay redefines the processor or a pipeline of the base. | Finding of the red team: `allow_all_keys: true`, a processor removed from `traces/in` or an overlay that overwrites it passed both collector checks. The canary tests (PLAN M1-69, M1-73) would see the effect only later. |
| M1-04/26 | 2026-10-11 | `slo-docs` derives the column Alert from `page_alert.disable` and `ticket_alert.disable`: `Page, Ticket`, `Ticket only`, `Page only` or `none`. It reads the `le` bound in each PromQL quoting (`le="x"`, `le='x'`, ``le=`x` ``, spaces around `=` allowed); a regex or negated `le` matcher (`=~`, `!=`, `!~`) is a finding, and then the column Threshold of that row is not compared. | Red team Minors 2 and 3 on d73c418: with the ticket alert disabled the correct row failed and the wrong row `Page, Ticket` passed, and `le='0.5'` read as no bound. A regex or negated matcher names no single bound, so the row cannot be checked; one finding says why instead of a second, misleading one about the cell. The cell values follow the table they are compared with. |
| M1-04/27 | 2026-10-11 | `tofu` counts a directory under `infra/tofu/modules/` as a module when it holds `.tf`, `.tf.json`, `.tofu` or `.tofu.json` files (`TOFU_FILES`). | OpenTofu reads all four forms ([OpenTofu files and directories](https://opentofu.org/docs/language/files/), retrieved 2026-10-12); a module made only of `.tofu` files was skipped and the check said `ok` (red team Minor 4). |
| M1-04/28 | 2026-10-11 | `alertmanager` maps the container path: amtool runs on a work copy of `infra/observability/alertmanager/` whose `/etc/alertmanager/` prefixes point into the copy, so `check-config` parses the templates; every `templates` entry must lie under `/etc/alertmanager/` (a relative entry counts from the directory of `alertmanager.yaml`) and match a file, else it is a finding. A file that is no YAML skips only this entry check; amtool reports it, and the route tests still run. Of the two options of the brief (map the path, or fail on an unmapped one) the mapping is taken, with the loud failure for every path outside the mount. | Red team Minor 5: a broken template named by its container path passed. Compose mounts exactly that directory there, so the mapping checks the normal form instead of forbidding it, and it is the cheaper option: it reuses the `rule_files` rule of `prometheus-config` (M1-04/17), now one helper `_mounted_files` for both. amtool accepts a pattern without a match, as promtool does. |
| M1-04/29 | 2026-10-11 | `collector` reports a reference to a config provider other than `env`, `file` and `yaml` (`COLLECTOR_PROVIDERS`; for example `${https:...}`, `${s3:...}`) and then runs no `validate`. Every opening `${<provider>:` counts, also one inside another reference (`${env:NAME:-${https:...}}`, which the collector resolves from the inside out); only YAML strings count, keys and values, not comments, and a file PyYAML cannot read is searched as text; `$$` is the escaped `$`. The finding says the provider is not allowed, because not every other provider reaches the network (`aes` decrypts locally with a key from the environment, [aesprovider README](https://github.com/open-telemetry/opentelemetry-collector-contrib/blob/443a0a020c3a61e5309ef3cedc83ea360d98a5fc/confmap/provider/aesprovider/README.md#L19), retrieved 2026-10-12). The docstring of `NO_NETWORK_PROXY` and `tools/opscheck/README.md` now say that the proxy stops only clients that honour the proxy variables. | Red team Minor 8: in a sandbox `${https:...}` tried a connection past the closed proxy, so the old text claimed more than the proxy does. The collector resolves references while it validates, so only a rule before `validate` keeps the gate off the network. A comment is no reference. The closing review of these fixes found the nested form and the file PyYAML rejects but the collector reads (both passed the first version). The Google Cloud Storage module source of `tofu` (`gcs::`) is not stopped by the proxy (red team run: it waited until `COMMAND_TIMEOUT_S`); an `s3::` source failed at the closed proxy in the closing review. The README says so; a rule for `gcs::` is not part of this follow-up. |
| M1-04/30 | 2026-10-11 | `main` handles SIGINT and SIGTERM alike for the run: the handler kills every running validator by its own process id, no validator starts after the stop (start and registration share one lock with the stop), `run_checks` removes its work directory, the exit status is 128 plus the signal number with the line `opscheck: stopped by <SIGNAL>` on stderr, and the previous handlers are restored afterwards (`SIG_DFL` where the previous handler was not set from Python). A killed validator is always waited for, and after a timeout its output is not read on, as `subprocess.run` did (the child is killed and waited for, [subprocess.run](https://docs.python.org/3/library/subprocess.html#subprocess.run), retrieved 2026-10-12). After SIGINT the script itself ends by SIGINT once it has cleaned up, as Python does after an uncaught KeyboardInterrupt ([What's New in Python 3.8](https://docs.python.org/3/whatsnew/3.8.html#other-language-changes), retrieved 2026-10-12), so a calling shell stops too; a stop that arrives during the removal of the work directory finishes the removal first. | Red team Minor 9: after SIGTERM the work directory `opscheck-*` stayed and a `tofu init` kept running. The red test showed a second gap: with SIGINT the thread pool of `collector` waited for its running validators, so the run did not stop within 20 s. The lock is reentrant and a stop that arrives while the main thread starts a process raises only after that process is registered and killed, so none escapes. |
| M1-04/31 | 2026-10-12 | Ported onto the English `main`: the tool reads the translated tables ("Further alerts", "Levels and routes", "Attributes in spans and logs", the columns `Alert`, `Level`, `Configuration value`, `Key`, `Default`, `Attribute`, the SLO columns `Target`, `Threshold`, `Alert` with the cells `none`, `Ticket only` and `Page only`), the threshold form is ``above `<name>` ``, the alert unit tests expect main's English annotation texts, and README, rulings, comments and test data are English. | All repository content is English (D-W-94). |

No gap in the core contract (P-11): 03 "Modules" still lists two modules without tables (`gateway`, `search`). |

The test prevents chapter and generator from drifting apart again. Source: go-arch-lint v1.19.0 builds the allowed project imports of a component only from its `mayDependOn` and the `commonComponents` ([allowed_project_imports.go](https://github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint/blob/v1.19.0/internal/services/spec/assembler/allowed_project_imports.go#L29-L33)), retrieved 2026-10-12. |

Source: go-arch-lint v1.19.0 rejects a `deps` entry without `mayDependOn`, `canUse` or an `any...` flag ([validator_deps.go](https://github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint/blob/v1.19.0/internal/services/spec/validator/validator_deps.go#L51-L61)), retrieved 2026-10-12. |
| M1-05/16 | 2026-10-11 | Every key and every string value in the register is a plain YAML string (tag `!!str`); `tools/archgen` rejects tagged keys (null, `!!binary`, `!!int`, `!!bool`, `!!timestamp`, the merge key `<<`) and tagged values with line, column and YAML path. Extended by M1-05/30 to plain scalars that YAML 1.1 reads differently. | Red Team finding: a `!!binary` key appeared with its text in the node walk and base64-decoded in the typed decode, and the checks saw a different entry than the one written (second instance after null keys). A `!!binary` value like `[!!binary Y2hhdA==]` silently became `chat`, and other readers of the register (M1-06) would see other names. The class is fixed in one place: only `!!str` counts. Source: YAML [binary type](https://yaml.org/type/binary.html) (`!!binary` is base64-encoded octets), retrieved 2026-10-12. |
| M1-05/17 | 2026-10-11 | A vendor pattern in the register is a Go import path: the first element is a lowercase domain with a dot, every further element consists of letters, digits, `-`, `_`, `~` and inner dots, `**` only as the last element and only after a domain and one more fixed element (`github.com/**` fails). `TestImportPathRule`, new cases in `TestRegisterRejects` and `TestArchLintVendorPatternIsLiteral` (end to end with a second library stub in the skeleton) hold this. | Red Team finding: go-arch-lint 1.19.0 translates a pattern into a regular expression and escapes only `.` and `/`; `github.com/jackc/pgx/v5\|` and `[^/]+/**` allowed every third-party import (red before the fix: archgen accepted them, go-arch-lint reported "No warnings found"). Apart from the escaped dot, the characters of a Go import path have no meaning in a regular expression. A pattern whose fixed part is only a hosting domain would allow almost all libraries (Minor 18 of the Red Team); the narrower rule requires at least a domain and one further element. Source: [02-architecture.md](spec/02-architecture.md#71-module-register-as-the-source), which cites the go-arch-lint v1.19.0 source, retrieved 2026-10-12. |

The directory check runs in `go generate` and thus in `make codegen-check`. | Red Team finding: with `exclude: [tools, in]` and a directory `in/`, all of `internal/` dropped out of the gate (go-arch-lint "No warnings found" despite a forbidden import), and `exclude: [tools]` silently took `toolsx/` along. An allow list for `exclude` would be narrower, but would need a list of its own for every skeleton; the prefix check hits exactly the error class. Source: [02-architecture.md](spec/02-architecture.md#71-module-register-as-the-source), which cites the go-arch-lint v1.19.0 source, retrieved 2026-10-12. |
| M1-05/19 | 2026-10-11 | `billing_mode_mismatch` and `tool_class_mismatch` have the error kind `invariant_violation`, not `conflict` as in the brief M1-05; the status stays `409`. `TestCodeKindsFollowBriefAndRulings` now names the kind of every code, `TestEveryCodeConstantIsInTable` checks every code constant against the table. | 07 section 4.1 assigns both reasons to `invariant_violation` (I3 at the start of a run, tool class above the invocation token), and the brief M1-28 expects the same kind; the brief M1-05 named `conflict` without a reason (Minor 5 of the Red Team). Because both kinds have `409`, 06 and the generated block of the problem codes stay consistent; other briefs stay unchanged. |
| M1-05/20 | 2026-10-11 | `(*errs.Error).PublicCode()` returns the code if it has a kind, otherwise `internal_error`, also for a nil `*Error`; `Error()` writes this public code. Responses (problem writer from M1-18) take the code from `PublicCode()` and the status from `Kind()`, never from the field `Code`; `LogValue` still writes the raw code. `Cause` carries `json:"-"`. | Minors 3 and 4 of the Red Team: an `*Error` from a struct literal had the kind `internal` (500), but its text and code stayed unknown (`bogus: d`), and `json.Marshal` wrote the internal cause. Code and kind now come from the same source; in the log the raw code stays visible, so that the cause can be found. |

| M1-05/22 | 2026-10-11 | Without the make feature `oneshell` (older GNU make, for example the one on macOS), the root `Makefile` sets `SHELL := bash -euo pipefail`; a make with `oneshell` uses `SHELL := bash` with `.SHELLFLAGS := -euo pipefail -c` unchanged. The explicit `\|\| exit 1` after every step stays mandatory (M1-01/8); `fmt-check` and the `semgrep` rule tests now have it too. `tools/ci/tests/test_make_shell.py` checks errexit, pipefail and nounset with the make first in `PATH`; the fake tests still also run with `bash -c` without flags. | Red Team finding (Major 2, Minor 33): make 3.81 ignores `.SHELLFLAGS`, so `fmt-check` ended with exit 0 on an unreadable Go file; it was the second instance after M1-01/8. make 3.81 calls `$(SHELL) -c <line>` and splits the SHELL value at spaces, so the flags arrive there (probe: `make -p` shows `SHELL := bash -euo pipefail` under 3.81, `SHELL := bash` under 4.4.1). Rejecting make 3.81 was ruled out, because the self-hosted runner and macOS use it. The class is fixed in one place; `make -n check` shows only the lines of `fmt-check`, `semgrep`, `licenses` and `arch` changed. Source: [GNU make NEWS](https://git.savannah.gnu.org/cgit/make.git/plain/NEWS), version 3.82: ".SHELLFLAGS" and ".ONESHELL" are new there, retrieved 2026-10-12. |
| M1-05/23 | 2026-10-11 | `make licenses` reads the allowlist through the variable `LICENSE_ALLOWLIST` (default `quality/licenses/allowed.txt`) and aborts with `licenses: <path> lists no license` if it names no license; lines of only spaces do not count. | Minor 24 of the Red Team: under make 3.81 an empty list became `--licenses=` and `--allowed_licenses=`, which no longer restrict the licenses, and the gate passed; under make 4 it ended without a message. Through the variable, tests check the case without changing the real file. Sources: go-licenses v2.0.1 falls back to rejecting only forbidden and unknown licenses when no allowed license is given ([check.go](https://github.com/google/go-licenses/blob/v2.0.1/check.go#L64-L68)); osv-scanner v2.6.0 treats an empty `--licenses` list as no allowlist and prints only a summary ([getters.go](https://github.com/google/osv-scanner/blob/v2.6.0/cmd/osv-scanner/internal/helper/getters.go#L17-L21)); both retrieved 2026-10-12. |

The working tree counts, because go-arch-lint and the compiler read it. Source: go-arch-lint v1.19.0 walks the module with [`filepath.WalkDir`](https://github.com/fe3dback/go-arch-lint/blob/v1.19.0/internal/services/project/scanner/scanner.go#L74), which [does not follow symbolic links](https://pkg.go.dev/path/filepath#WalkDir), retrieved 2026-10-12. |
