On that tree the `coverage` gate fails: `services/server` reaches 79.0 % against the floor of 80 %, because the provider and identity code of `identity/app` and `identity/adapters/postgres` is exercised only by the HTTP tests of another package, which per-package coverage does not count. The integration adds tests in those two packages (`adapters/postgres/providers_test.go`, `app/manage_test.go`, more cases in `app/identities_test.go`) that run every store method and the provider and identity use cases against PostgreSQL; production code is unchanged. | Blocker of the integration: the gate also runs on GitHub CI. The ticket's own `make -k check` skipped `coverage` because its `test` target failed on an unrelated timing flake. Test-only fix, no change of ticket behaviour. |

## M1-44 API tokens with prefix and checksum

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-44/1 | 2026-10-13 | Base `int/m1-wave10` at `5cd89cd`, branch `feat/M1-44-identity-api-tokens` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally, the integration lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in German chapters and READMEs. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and language (as M1-35/1). |
| M1-44/2 | 2026-10-13 | A personal token exists only for the person itself: the authz guard of `token.create` (07 section 4.2 step 9) refuses a holder of kind `user` other than the caller with `forbidden not_self`; the application layer reads the holder's kind from `PrincipalSource` into `domain.TokenFacts`. An unknown holder passes the guard, so the use case answers `404`. | The brief's ruling: a personal token is the person's own credential; an owner minting one for someone else could act as that person. |
| M1-44/3 | 2026-10-13 | The requirement of `token.create` is `Any(Owner, Self)`, `token.list` and `token.revoke` keep `Any(Self, Owner)`. When the requirement of `token.create` fails for a holder that is a person other than the caller, the reason is `not_self` (`domain.notSelfForPersonalToken`); for an agent or app holder it is the reason of the first atom, `owner_only` (also for the owner's personal token, SR5). | The brief requires `not_self` for a personal token of another person (08 section 12) and `owner_only` for `TestPersonalTokenOfOwnerHasNoOwnerRights`; `Any` reports the reason of its first alternative (M1-28). Red team round 1, minor 1. |
| M1-44/4 | 2026-10-13 | Personal tokens carry no scopes (`/scopes` must be empty for `pat` as for `agt`). | The brief's ruling: personal tokens act with the person's grants; 07 knows scopes only for apps. |
| M1-44/5 | 2026-10-13 | A found but revoked or expired token is not refused by the Authenticator: it returns the actor of the token's principal with the token id and `CredentialInactive` (new field of `kernel/actor.Actor`), and `authorize` refuses it in `check_active` with `401 unauthenticated` and the audit reason `token_revoked`. The Idempotency-Key stage (`platform/httpserver`), which runs before `authorize`, neither claims nor replays for such an actor. No DPoP proof is checked for an inactive token and `last_used_at` is not written. A missing token, a kind mismatch and every format failure are refused by the Authenticator (`401 unauthenticated`, no audit). | The brief asks for the audit reason `token_revoked`; only authorize knows the action of the request and audits a denial (07 section 4.2), as M1-34 does for sessions. Red team round 1 (major): without the flag the stage replayed a stored answer to a revoked or expired token, and to a revoked DPoP-bound token without its key. The two files outside the brief's scope (`kernel/actor`, `platform/httpserver`) change by one field and one condition. |
| M1-44/6 | 2026-10-13 | Revoking a token is a rights change: `revokeToken`, `Tokens.Revoke` outside a unit and every caller of `RevokeAllForPrincipal` raise `permission_version`; `Revoke` inside a unit that is no rights change and `RevokeAllForPrincipal` outside one fail with `internal_error`. `access.changed` is appended only where the M1-29 path computes a difference; a token changes no grant, so a revocation appends none. | authz caches token liveness with the rights snapshot per version (M1-28/12); without the new version a revoked token would stay live in the cache of every process. 05 marks `token.revoke` as a rights change; the brief says "as the path computes it". |
| M1-44/7 | 2026-10-13 | `revokeToken` authorizes in a first unit of work and revokes in a second one declared as the rights change; an already revoked token ends after the first with `204` and no new entry. | A refused or empty call must not raise the rights version; the second unit locks the row, so two concurrent revocations write one entry. |
| M1-44/8 | 2026-10-13 | `PersonalTokenSwitch` is bound to `app.PersonalTokensDefault` (always off) in `internal/wire` until M1-47 registers `tokens.personal_enabled` and rebinds it over `instance.Flags`; `wire.IdentityWrites.PersonalTokens` is the seam. Tests use a fake switch for both values. | The brief's binding; 16 section 6.2: an unregistered flag is off. |
| M1-44/9 | 2026-10-13 | `createToken` for an app answers `409 conflict app_not_installed` before the body is checked. | Apps cannot exist in M1; M2-24 replaces the answer with the installation check (brief). |
| M1-44/10 | 2026-10-13 | Besides the port `Tokens` of 02 section 4.3 the root package declares `TokenAdmin`, the use cases of the three operations (they authorize, `Tokens` does not). | As `People` beside `Directory` (M1-31): other modules use `Tokens` without an authorization of their own action, the handlers use `TokenAdmin`. The port name of 02 is unchanged. |
| M1-44/11 | 2026-10-13 | `auth.dpop_required_for` is a string list with the default `["agent"]` (the JSON form, so the chapter cell and the schema agree) and the cross-check `dpop_required_for`, which refuses values outside `agent`, `app` and `personal` and names only the key. | The brief asks to register the allowed values; the configuration kinds have no enum list. |
| M1-44/12 | 2026-10-13 | `Parse` accepts the kinds `agt`, `app`, `pat` and `whi` only; `hpc` (host pairing code, ADR 0061 addendum, 24 section 3.2) stays with the gitleaks rule and its own ticket, and `whi` is never accepted by the Authenticator. | Brief M1-44 lists four kinds; pairing codes are no API tokens of `createToken`. |
| M1-44/13 | 2026-10-13 | The tests of ID-T23 and the operations run in the session world of M1-34 (`sessions_env_test.go` with the token handlers and a fake `PersonalTokenSwitch`); step-up comes from the M1-35 flow. The cases run as named subtests of three groups (`TestAPITokens_Issue`, `TestAPITokens_Authenticate`, `TestAPITokens_Lifecycle`) with one world per group: a world costs seconds to build and costs only tenths per case, and with one world per case the package passed the default 10 minute test timeout of `make test` on a loaded machine. The code of the earlier session existed before these tests; mutation runs (format check after the lookup, no `Bearer` refusal of bound tokens, revocation without the rights change) turned `TestIDT23_WrongChecksumRejectedWithoutLookup`, `TestIDT23_DPoPBoundAgentTokenWithoutProof` and `TestRevokeToken_Immediate` red. | Test first could not be shown for code written before the session was lost; the mutations prove the tests catch the behaviour. |
| M1-44/14 | 2026-10-13 | The token examples of `docs/api/openapi.yaml` (`listTokens`, `createToken`, `ApiToken`, `ApiTokenCreated`) use a valid 60 character token with the marker `EXAMPLE` (the first checksum vector), the 6 character prefix `bothy_agt_EXAMPL`, `scopes: []`, `dpop_jkt` and `expires_at`; the server stub and the TypeScript client are regenerated. | Red team round 1, minor 3: the old examples were no valid tokens and showed scopes an agent token cannot have; the brief allows changing openapi.yaml where the implementation proves it wrong. |
| M1-44/15 | 2026-10-13 | Not changed after red team round 1: the table grant `UPDATE` of `bothy_app` on `api_tokens` (as every comparable table, 16 allows DML), `apitoken.Parsed` without redaction (never logged; it holds what the caller already has), no cross-check `auth.personal_token_max_ttl <= auth.api_token_max_ttl` (08 does not require it), no second authorization in the revoking unit of `revokeToken` (the window only decides whether one allow entry is written). The expiry is now cut to microseconds before the check (nit 5). | Nits of red team round 1 with their reason. |
| M1-44/16 | 2026-10-13 | An actor with `CredentialInactive` is keyed by the client IP in `ratelimit.RequestKey` (as the anonymous actor) and settles the failed-credentials precheck as `401 unauthenticated`, so its `rl.public_auth` token stays spent. This replaces the acceptance of the rate limiter point in M1-44/15. | Red team round 2 (major, reproduced): a revoked or expired token, also a revoked DPoP-bound token without its key, emptied the buckets of its principal with 429 for every live credential, and an expired row never goes away. The two stages run before authorize; the change is one condition each. |
| M1-44/17 | 2026-10-13 | The API token store of `adapters/postgres` and the port `identity.Tokens` get their own tests in their packages (`TestAPITokens_StoreRoundTrip`, `TestTokens_Port`); a `token_hash` collision on insert stays `internal_error` like a reused primary key, and the doc comment of `InsertAPIToken` says so. | `make coverage` counts each package by its own tests only, and the HTTP tests of `adapters/httpapi` left the server module under its threshold; the new store test showed that the comment promised a conflict the error mapping never gave. |
| M1-44/18 | 2026-10-15 | Port onto main: `(*server).handlers` in `internal/wire/serve.go` fills the identity handlers through `(*Identity).fillHandlers` and the generic `fill`, which sets a nil handler to the built one; an injected handler stays and nothing is filled before its module started (`TestHandlers_FillsBuiltAndKeepsInjected`). | On main the function also fills the signing key handlers of M1-32; with the `Tokens` branch of this ticket it reached cognitive complexity 16 (gocognit, limit 15). The integration branch made the same change as INT-W12/3 one ticket later; moving the repeated nil checks into one helper keeps the behaviour. |

## M1-50 Audit verification, checkpoints and queries

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-50/1 | 2026-10-13 | Base `int/m1-wave10` at `5cd89cd`, branch `feat/M1-50-audit-verify` in its own worktree; the branch is not merged locally and the worktree stays, the PR lane ports it. Text this ticket adds is English, also in German chapters and READMEs (their German parts stay). | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step and the brief's language (as M1-35/1). |
| M1-50/2 | 2026-10-13 | A break that `audit.chain_verify` detects ends the run without error and without a checkpoint: one more `bothy.audit.verify.failures`, an `audit.verify` entry with decision `error`, no actor and `{first_broken_seq, failure_reason}`, and the log line `audit chain verification failed`; River does not retry it. Storage, signing and sink errors fail the run, and River retries. See [02 section 12.3](spec/02-architecture.md) and [19 SEC-AUD-7](spec/19-security.md). | A retry cannot repair a broken chain and would only repeat the entry; the alert `BothyAuditChainBroken` on the counter takes over (brief "Job" item 2). |
| M1-50/3 | 2026-10-13 | The file sink writes `<path>/<instance_id>/<seq with 20 digits>.jws` with directory mode `0700` and file mode `0600` through a temporary file in the same directory (`O_EXCL`, synced), then a hard link to the name, then `fsync` of the directory; 19 SEC-AUD-7 said "rename" and now says link. `<path>` must exist; the sink never creates it. The same content again is accepted, other content fails and the file stays. See [19 SEC-AUD-7](spec/19-security.md). | A rename replaces an existing name (POSIX), so two writers of one seq could overwrite each other; `link` fails when the name exists (`TestSinkConcurrentPut`). A missing mount must not silently put the checkpoints on the database volume. |
| M1-50/4 | 2026-10-13 | `exportAuditEvents` streams the contiguous `seq` span from the smallest to the largest `seq` whose `occurred_at` lies in [`from`, `to`), including a row inside the span whose `occurred_at` lies outside it; the description in openapi.yaml says so. An error after the first line aborts the response (`http.ErrAbortHandler`). | An aggregated denial keeps the time of its first denial (05 "2.4"), so a strict time filter could drop a row from the middle and break the `prev_hash` link that makes the export verifiable offline (19 SEC-AUD-4); an aborted transfer is visible, a short complete-looking stream would not be. |
| M1-50/5 | 2026-10-13 | `verifyAuditChain` reports the failure with the smallest `seq` among the row failures of the walk, the first invalid sink checkpoint (at any `seq`, also outside the range) and a trusted sink checkpoint whose row is gone (below the first retained row when the range starts there, above the head when it ends there; `checkpoint_mismatch`). `from_seq` and `to_seq` below 1 and `from_seq` above `to_seq` (also after capping at the head) are `400 validation_failed`; a `from_seq` below the first retained row is raised to it. `last_checkpoint_seq` is the newest trusted sink checkpoint. | The brief's "first failure wins" needs one order; `seq` is the order of the chain. Deleting the newest or oldest rows must not pass as a shorter intact chain while the sink holds a checkpoint over them. |
| M1-50/6 | 2026-10-13 | The job walks only the rows after the last checkpoint of `audit_checkpoints`, after checking that checkpoint: its JWS verifies with a key of purpose `audit_checkpoint`, its payload names this instance and the stored `seq` and `hash`, and its row still has that hash. Without a sink checkpoints are still signed into the database and go to the sink once one is configured. | SEC-AUD-2 recomputes "the segment since the last checkpoint"; the checks keep a changed database checkpoint from becoming the trusted start. Older rows are compared by `verifyAuditChain` against the sink (README "Limits of the verification"). |
| M1-50/7 | 2026-10-13 | The insert of a checkpoint takes an advisory transaction lock and reads the last checkpoint again; when it changed since the walk, the run inserts nothing. | Two processes with the role `all` could otherwise sign two checkpoints that link the same predecessor, a fork that the sink would report as `checkpoint_invalid`. |
| M1-50/8 | 2026-10-13 | The route table gets `Download` (a 2xx response with a media type that is neither `application/json` nor a `+json` type); `platform/httpserver` gives such a route the deadline `http.transfer_timeout` for the context and the write, like an upload. Of the enabled operations only `exportAuditEvents` is one. | The brief puts the export into "the route class of downloads, deadline `http.transfer_timeout`" (02 14.2), and the class was so far derived from the request body only. |
| M1-50/9 | 2026-10-13 | `instance.Keys.Verify(ctx, purpose, compactJWS)` is added to the port; it wraps every failure about the JWS in `instance.ErrJWSInvalid` and leaves the check of `typ` to the caller (the audit module requires `audit-checkpoint+json`). | The brief adds the method when the base lacks it; a database error must stay `503`/`500`, not become `checkpoint_invalid`. |
| M1-50/10 | 2026-10-13 | A stored row whose `details` is no JSON object or whose `prev_hash` or `hash` has another size reads with nil details or a zero hash instead of failing the read, so the walk reports `hash_mismatch` or `link_mismatch` at that row. | Such a row can only come from a change outside the server; an error would turn a detected tampering into a `500`. |
| M1-50/11 | 2026-10-13 | Audit details of the operations: `listAuditEvents` names the filters that are set (`actor_id`, `action`, `decision`, `resource_kind`, `resource_id`, `from`, `to`), `exportAuditEvents` `{from, to}`, `verifyAuditChain` `{from_seq, to_seq, ok}`; the recorder adds `ip_hmac` and `ip_key_id` as for every entry with a client IP. The filters `action` and `resource_kind` must be codes (`400 validation_failed`). The entry of a read is written in a unit of work of its own before the page or the stream is returned. | SEC-AUD-5 allows IDs, codes and times only; a filter that is no code cannot match a stored row anyway. A read whose entry cannot be written is not served. |
| M1-50/12 | 2026-10-13 | The response validator of `httpservertest` checks an `application/x-ndjson` body line by line against the schema of the media type. | The library reads a response body as one JSON value; every export would otherwise fail the contract check of the handler tests. |
| M1-50/13 | 2026-10-13 | The log line of a detected break carries `error.type` `audit_chain_broken` and `bothy.reason` with the failure reason, not the `seq`. | The attribute catalogue has no key for a sequence number (as M1-24); the `audit.verify` entry of the run names it. |
| M1-50/14 | 2026-10-13 | The port types are `audit.Query`, `audit.Page` and `audit.EventWriter`, not the brief's `AuditQuery`, `AuditPage` and `AuditEventWriter`. | The lint rule `exported` of revive refuses names that stutter with the package name (`audit.AuditQuery`); the meaning is the brief's. |
| M1-50/15 | 2026-10-13 | Red team round 1 (`NEIN`), all fixed with a test each: `listAuditEvents` writes every item as `EventJSON` (canonical row plus `hash`, three fractional digits), `TestListItemsAreCanonical`; until the anchors of M7 every walk that starts at the first retained row starts at row 1, so deleted oldest rows are `seq_gap` at 1 (`TestSECT16_DeletedOldestRowsAreDetected`; this replaces the trust in the first retained row of M1-50/5); the walk reads the stored hash of a row through `Rows`, so a hash of another size is a detected break, not a storage error (`TestWrongSizeHashIsABreak`); `verifyAuditChain` returns its result and the job ends without retry when the audit entry cannot be written, which is reported as `audit entry of the chain verification not written` (`TestDeletedNewestRowsStillAnswer`); the `to` filter has a test (`TestListToFilter`); the use cases have tests in their own package (`internal/audit/app/log_test.go`), which keep the Go coverage gate above its threshold. | A deleted newest row breaks the audit hook of M1-24 for every new entry; hiding the verification result from the owner then would hide the break itself. |
| M1-50/16 | 2026-10-13 | With a sink, every job run first reads the sink and reports `checkpoint_mismatch` at a sink checkpoint newer than the last checkpoint of `audit_checkpoints` (lost database checkpoints); every unexported checkpoint is checked like the last one (signature, payload, row) before it goes to the sink, else `checkpoint_invalid`; a sink that holds other content under the seq (`app.ErrCheckpointConflict`) is `checkpoint_mismatch`. All three are breaks (no checkpoint, counter, entry, no retry). A sink that cannot be read fails the run before it signs. | Red team round 1, minors: a deleted database checkpoint let the job fork or freeze the sink without a count, and a forged row would have been exported. |
| M1-50/17 | 2026-10-13 | The file sink refuses a link in place of the directory of the instance or of a checkpoint file, narrows a wider mode of the directory to `0700` and syncs the root after creating the directory; `ParseSink` takes exactly `file://` with an absolute path and refuses percent escapes and control characters. | Red team round 1, minors (`TestSinkRefusesLinksAndWideModes`, `TestParseSink`). |
| M1-50/18 | 2026-10-13 | Red team round 2 (`NEIN`), both fixed with a test each: the job reads the sink before the last checkpoint of `audit_checkpoints`, and the export reads the head after every batch of unexported checkpoints, so a run that overlaps the signing or the export of another run is no break (`TestLog_OverlappingRunsAreNoBreak`, `TestLog_OverlappingExportIsNoBreak`, the latter from the re-review of round 2); a row with a `seq` below 1 is `seq_gap` at that `seq` in the verification and the job (`TestLog_RowBelowOneIsABreak`). No `CHECK (seq >= 1)` on `audit_events`: the detection does not depend on it, and a role that can insert rows can append a correctly linked forged row at the head anyway. | A checkpoint reaches the sink only after its row committed, so a database read after the sink listing sees every listed checkpoint unless it was deleted; the walk starts at row 1, so a row below 1 was never checked and must not pass as checked. |

| INT-W13/4 | 2026-10-13 | Test code only: the helper `instanceRow(done bool)` of M1-46 in `identity/adapters/httpapi` is renamed `settingsRow`, because M1-37 declares `instanceRow()` on the same world; the settings tests call `callH` of M1-44 instead of `callWith`, which M1-37 replaced by `postWith`; `loginCfg` of the M1-37 login tests sets the API token limits that M1-44 made required for `NewIdentity`. | Each ticket compiled alone; the merged package declared one method twice, called a removed helper and built identity with a configuration `app.NewTokens` refuses. The helpers do the same thing, so the tests keep their meaning. |
| INT-W13/5 | 2026-10-13 | `wire.Setup` (the assembly of `bothy setup`) builds the instance settings with `NewInstanceSettings` over its pool, unit of work, Authorizer, recorder and appender and passes `Settings` in `IdentityWrites`, as the start step `settings` does for `serve`. | Semantic conflict: M1-46 made `instance.Settings` a required collaborator of `NewPeople`, and M1-37's `bothy setup` built identity without it, so every `bothy setup` command exited 1 with `NewPeople needs every collaborator` (reproduced by `TestSetupInvalidArgumentsAndInfrastructure` and the login tests of `identity/app`, green after the fix). |
| INT-W13/6 | 2026-10-13 | Test code only: the two wrong-case token literals of the M1-44 test `domain/apitoken/apitoken_test.go` (`HasPrefix` with a capitalised prefix and the case "upper case prefix" of `Parse`) are built with `strings.ToUpper` from `apitoken.Prefix` and the valid token instead of writing the working name in another case. | Semantic conflict: the check of the working name's places that M1-46 added (`tools/ci/tests/test_product_name_places.py`) allows only the forms of 02 section 6, and the M1-44 test on the base wrote `Bothy_agt_x` and `BOTHY`, so `make test` was red on the merged tree although each ticket passed alone (found by the integration review). The test keeps its inputs and meaning. |

## M1-38 Device approval and pairing

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M1-38/1 | 2026-10-14 | Base `int/m1-wave13` at `82693e5`, branch `feat/M1-38-identity-device-approval` in its own worktree; not merged locally and not pushed, the PR lane ports it. Migration version 18 is the next free one on the base. | Instructions of the launcher, which override the brief's merge step (as M1-37/1). |
| M1-38/2 | 2026-10-14 | A wrong verification code answers `200` with the device in state `revoked` (`revoke_reason` `verification_failed`), audited as allow `device.approve` with `result: rejected_wrong_code`. See [08 section 5.2](spec/08-identity.md). | The request had a committed effect (the device and its sessions are revoked); an error status would claim nothing changed, and the approving person must see that the device is gone. |
| M1-38/3 | 2026-10-14 | The verification code reaches the pending device through the `TokenResponse` of the login that leaves it pending and through `getDevice` of its own id. `listMyDevices` from a pending device stays `403 device_pending`, because authz narrows `device.list` of a pending device to the resource of the device itself (M1-28, 05 section 2.5); the use case still sets the code on the own entry, so the description of `Device.verification_code` holds once a pending device may list. Other devices and owners never get it. | The authz rule is normative and older; the device needs only one way to read its code. |
| M1-38/4 | 2026-10-14 | Pairing codes: 8 Crockford characters from one `crypto/rand` byte each (low five bits); the insert uses `ON CONFLICT (pairing_code_hash) WHERE status = 'requested' DO NOTHING`, and a collision draws a new code, at most 5 times (`pairingCodeAttempts`), then `500`. | 40 bits against few open pairings make a collision rare; several in a row mean a broken random source, which must not loop. The partial unique index only covers requested pairings, so a closed pairing frees its code. |
| M1-38/5 | 2026-10-14 | `completePairing` with a proof of another key rejects a `requested` pairing (committed, then `401 invalid_dpop_proof`); an `approved` pairing answers the same `401` and stays approved. | The state machine of the brief has no `approved -> rejected`; the device exists for the approving person, only its own key can collect it, and the collection window ends it otherwise (`pairing_expired`). |
| M1-38/6 | 2026-10-14 | `approveDevice` and `approvePairing` write in a unit of work declared as the rights change `device.approve` of the device's person (the caller for a pairing), the expiries as `device.expire`; like `addOwner`, a first read and `authorize` run before that unit, and the unit locks and checks the row again. An unknown pairing code ends the step-up and writes its deny entry in a committed unit without rights change. | 05 marks both actions as rights changes; the rights hook must lock `permission_version` before any domain row, and a refused or unknown request must not raise the rights version. The deny entry belongs to the committed effect (the ended step-up), so it is recorded in the unit, not aggregated after a rollback. |
| M1-38/7 | 2026-10-14 | An expiry found on access (an expired pending device at `approveDevice`, an expired pairing at `completePairing`) is written in its own unit of work with the anonymous principal, so it is audited and evented like the job: the system action `device.expire` without actor, `revoked_by` NULL. The request still answers `409 invalid_state_transition` or `410 gone`. | The brief applies the expiry "lazily on access and by the cleanup job" and audits it as a system action with actor NULL; the caller did not decide the revocation. |
| M1-38/8 | 2026-10-14 | `devices.created_at` (and `approved_at` of an active device) takes the clock of the login or pairing request; `Devices.Register` keeps the database time. A pending device past `auth.device_pending_ttl` counts as revoked in the Authenticator (`401 session_revoked`, `device_inactive`), in `refreshToken` and as a known device of a later login (`409`). | The pending window counts from the request clock the use cases compare with; correctness must not wait for the job (brief "Correctness never depends on the job"). |
| M1-38/9 | 2026-10-14 | Cleanup job case: `identity.auth_cleanup` existed (M1-35); `AuthCleanup.Run` now also calls `ExpirePendingDevices` and `ExpirePairings` of the login service, each device or pairing in its own unit of work, read in batches of 200. Expired pairings are marked `expired`, not deleted. | One job per 02 section 12.3; separate units keep one failing row from blocking the rest, and deleting old transient rows belongs to the retention job, which grants its delete. |
| M1-38/10 | 2026-10-14 | Red team round 1 (`NEIN`), fixed with tests: the units that must commit although the request answers an error run on `txn.Detach` (`committed` in `app/approval.go`): the end of the step-up and its deny entry after an unknown pairing code, the expiry of a pending device found by `approveDevice`, and the unit of `completePairing` with its rejection and expiry. The approval tests send an `Idempotency-Key` like a client (`approveDevice` and `approvePairing` are keyed), and the wrong-code case runs once with and once without the key. | With the key the Idempotency-Key stage runs the use case inside its own unit of work and rolls back every error answer, so a wrong pairing code ended neither the step-up nor left a deny entry, and a guess cost no new biometric confirmation (reproduced by the red team, `TestPairingWrongCodeEndsStepUp` red before the fix). |
| M1-38/11 | 2026-10-15 | Port onto main: the migration keeps the number 00018 of the integration branch (INT-W14/2), its comments name the English chapter files (new checksum recorded with `make migrations-lock`), and the code comments, the configuration test and these rulings name the English chapter files. None of the other integration fixes of `int/m1-wave14` touches this ticket alone: they join it with M1-40, M1-41, M1-43, M1-45, M1-47, M1-48 and M1-51, and `api/rest/rest.gen.go` stays below `repo.max_file_kb` with this ticket (the split of INT-W14/4 comes with M1-41). | `main` already holds M1-37 (`00016`) and M1-46 (`00017`), as the integration branch did, so the number follows without a gap; the specification is English on main. No behaviour changes. |

## M2-01 Worker consumers of the event log

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M2-01-1 | 2026-10-12 | No migration seeds `event_consumer_cursors`. In the roles `worker` and `all`, `ConsumerService.Ensure` inserts the row of every registered consumer in one unit of work before the runner starts, with `INSERT ... SELECT $1, COALESCE(max(seq), 0) FROM event_log ON CONFLICT (name) DO NOTHING`; unregistered names have no row and no lag series. The column default `0` of 03 stays. Place: `00007_event_consumer_cursors.sql`, `queries/consumers.sql`, 02 11.5 item 5. | A seeded row for a consumer that arrives in a later release (`push`, M3-19) would make it replay every event retained since the migration; a new consumer starts at the head like the first connection of an agent consumer (04 15.1 item 2). Reading `max(seq)` in one statement is safe because a reader that sees `seq = k` sees every smaller number (04 3.2 item 3); two workers starting together run the same statement and one row wins. |
| D-M2-01-2 | 2026-10-12 | `domain.PlanRound`: numbers contiguous from `last_seq + 1` are processed; when `last_seq + 1` lies below `min(seq)` and the first number read equals `min(seq)`, the events in between were removed by retention, the round processes from `min(seq)`, logs `event consumer lost events to retention` once at error level with `bothy.consumer.name` and `bothy.event.seq` and continues; every other jump, hole or repeat is `ErrConsumerGap`, the round rolls back and the consumer stalls. `min(seq)` is read in the round's transaction only when the first number does not follow the cursor. Place: `domain/consumer.go`, `app/consumers.go`, 02 11.5 item 6. | The removed events cannot come back and stalling would not help; a webhook subscription detects its own gap through `last_delivered_seq` (11 6), and the alarm fires long before. 04 3.2 items 3 and 4 rule out other holes, so they are bugs; unlike the tail's `ErrGap` the worker does not exit, the cursor just never passes the hole. The round holds `ACCESS SHARE` on `event_log`, so no partition is detached between the reads. |
| D-M2-01-3 | 2026-10-12 | A handler error, a handler panic (recovered into kind internal, the panic value is not kept), a database error and `ErrConsumerGap` roll back the whole round: no job, no cursor move, the next attempt at the next wake or poll; no event is ever skipped. The runner logs the first failure after a success at error level (`event consumer round failed`, `bothy.consumer.name`, `error.type` as SQLSTATE, `consumer_gap`, `timeout`, `canceled` or the `kernel/errs` kind) and the next success at info level (`event consumer recovered`). Each consumer has its own goroutine, so a stalled one never holds back another. Place: `app/consumer_runner.go`, 02 11.5 item 7. | One failing event must stall only its consumer, visibly through the gauge and the alarm, rather than lose an event; edge-triggered lines keep a database outage, during which the worker pauses (02 14.4), from flooding the log. |
| D-M2-01-4 | 2026-10-12 | Before the cursor moves, the round asks the appender of `sync/app` (its per-unit `txn.Key` buffer, `bufferedEvents`) whether the unit buffered durable events; if so it fails with kind internal and the unit rolls back. Place: `app/appender.go`, `app/consumers.go`, 02 11.5 item 8. | The consumer would read its own output, and the round would take the `event_seq` counter lock and lengthen the serialized commit phase (02 11.7); ADR 0051 lets a round insert jobs only. |
| D-M2-01-5 | 2026-10-12 | Two observable gauges, `bothy.event.consumer.lag` (`{event}`, `head_seq - last_seq`, floored at zero) and `bothy.event.consumer.pending_age` (`s`, now from `kernel/clock` minus `occurred_at` of the oldest event after the cursor, zero at the head), with the only attribute `bothy.consumer.name` from the closed set `sync.ConsumerNames()`; one callback calls `Consumers.States` (one read of all rows with the head, then one read per row, bounded by `database.statement_timeout`), and a failed read drops the points. Every role registers them in start step 6b. The registry `internal/platform/otel/metrics` of M1-68 is not merged, so they live in `internal/sync/adapters/otelmetrics` with the meter of the global provider; `sync` already had the `otel` vendor group. `bothy.consumer.name` is in `otel/attr`, the collector allowlist and signals.md 5.3. Place: `adapters/otelmetrics/consumers.go`, signals.md 4.2. | The plan row asks for `head_seq` minus `last_seq`, but `sync.event_retention` is a time window, so the alarm needs the age too. A stopped or missing worker must still show a growing lag, so the api role exports it as well; telemetry never blocks (17 rule 4). |
| D-M2-01-6 | 2026-10-12 | Alarm `BothyEventConsumerLag`, severity `page`, `max by (bothy_consumer_name) (bothy_event_consumer_pending_age_seconds{job="bothy/bothy-server"}) > 900` for `5m`, on the new key `consumer.pending_alert_after` (place Server, duration, default `15m`, at least `1s`). The start check `consumer_alert_below_retention` requires it strictly below `sync.event_retention` and names both keys and no value. The rule file holds the number; `alert-thresholds` keeps it equal to the default. Place: `bothy-alerts.yaml`, alerting.md 3.2 and 4, 02 13 item 4 and 22, `configkeys_consumer.go`, `configchecks.go`. | A normal worker restart takes at most `shutdown.total_timeout` (`60s`) plus a few `consumer.poll_interval` (`1s`), far below `15m`, and the `for` of the neighbouring lag alarm adds five minutes; `15m` is far below the `30d` retention, so the operator has weeks before any event is lost. `page`, because a standing consumer stops pushes and outgoing webhooks, which people notice at once (alerting.md 1). |
| D-M2-01-7 | 2026-10-12 | One `LISTEN event_log` connection per process for all worker consumers, opened with `pg.Pool.DialDedicated(ctx, "bothy-consumer-wake")`, apart from the tail's connection; the listening loop of the M1-16 tail moved into the shared type `listener` in `adapters/postgres`, which both use. A notification wakes every consumer through a channel of capacity one with a non-blocking send; the payload is not read. The connection is reconnected with exponential backoff from `consumer.poll_interval` up to `database.listen_reconnect_backoff_cap`, and every consumer also polls every `consumer.poll_interval`. Without a registered consumer the runner starts nothing and opens no connection. Place: `adapters/postgres/listen.go`, `waker.go`, 02 11.5 item 9. | In role `all` the ordered loop of the tail must never depend on the consumers; polling covers a lost connection and nothing is lost because the cursor lives in the database (02 14.4). The production list is empty in M2-01, and an idle connection per worker would serve nobody. |
| D-M2-01-8 | 2026-10-12 | After a full batch (`consumer.batch_size` events) the next round of that consumer starts at once (drain); after a short batch, an idle round, a locked row or a failed round the consumer waits for the next wake or poll. Place: `app/consumer_runner.go`. | A backlog drains without waiting a poll per batch, while `consumer.batch_size` keeps each round, and with it the `ACCESS SHARE` lock that a detach of the partition maintenance waits for, short. |
| D-M2-01-9 | 2026-10-12 | The stop step `event_consumers` lies in `lifecycle.PhaseConsumers` (before River's phase) with budget `0`, the rest of `shutdown.total_timeout`: the runner starts no new round and waits for the running ones; when the budget runs out the round's context is cancelled and the round rolls back. An unexpected end of the runner (the waker ends early, a panic outside a handler) goes through `Failed()` to `lifecycle.Runner.FailOn`, and the process exits `1` like after an unexpected stop of the River client. Place: `wire/sync.go`, `wire/serve.go`, 02 14.5, 15.2 and 15.3. | 02 15.3 gives this step no own budget; either way no job is lost or doubled, because cursor and jobs commit together. A process whose consumers ended would look healthy while pushes and webhooks stop. |
| D-M2-01-10 | 2026-10-12 | Branch `feat/M2-01-worker-event-consumers` in its own worktree from the head of `int/m1-wave6` (`785916c`); this session does not merge it locally and does not remove the worktree, the PR lane ports it. New text in the German chapters (02, alerting.md, signals.md, ADR 0051, threat model) is written in English. The tests take two hooks of `wire.Deps`: `ConsumerHandlers` (replaces the empty production list in worker and all) and `ConsumerGauges` (replaces the registration on the global meter provider), because `otel.Setup` installs one global provider per process and parallel serve tests would overwrite it. | The launcher's task overrides the merge step of the brief ("Definition of done") and requires English output. The hooks follow the pattern of `Deps.Registrations` and let the serve tests prove that worker and all run a registered consumer and that role api exports the gauges without a runner. |
| D-M2-01-11 | 2026-10-13 | Port onto main: the migration is `00009_event_consumer_cursors.sql` (00007 and 00008 are M1-20 and M1-33), the start step `identity` of M1-33 stands in `components()` right after `authz` and before `routes`, held by `TestStartSequenceModuleSteps`, and `closeTimeout` lives only in `listen.go`, which the log tail and the waker share. | M2-01 rebuilt the start sequence as `components()`; a step left in the old list would start in no role, and the device handlers must exist when the routes are mounted. The same renumbering as the integration (ADR 0007 rule 4). |

## M4-03 Accent variants

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-03-0 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win, and text this ticket adds is English. The tool's ruff configuration selects no `D` rules; the new code carries docstrings anyway, no lint rule is added. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request. |
| D-M4-03-1 | 2026-10-14 | The rules member `accent_variants` of `design/design-rules.json` names the default (`spruce`), the seven accents of 01 section 12 and the eight swapped roles. `tokenkit/accents.py` validates it (default in the accents, every accent a palette scale, every role an opaque colour in both themes) and raises a `RuleError` naming the member. A role is swapped in a theme only where it aliases `palette.spruce.<step>` directly; otherwise it is kept and shown as "not swapped". | One place for the list the server, the apps and the gate share; a role that does not follow the brand scale (dark `color.focus.ring` on `gorse`) must not change with the accent. |
| D-M4-03-2 | 2026-10-14 | Step search: a variant starts on the same step of the chosen scale (nearest step by number if missing, a tie goes darker in light and lighter in dark) and, while one of its pairings fails, moves one step towards more contrast (darker when the role is the darker colour of the failing pair, else lighter) until all pass; a scale end, a flat scale or a revisited step fails the build with `accent <a>, theme <t>: no step of <scale> meets <pairing>`. `moved_from` records the start step and `contrast.md` shows it. With today's palette no variant moves. | A fixed step on a lighter scale cannot always meet AA, while 01 section 12 promises that every listed accent works; the moved steps stay visible. |
| D-M4-03-3 | 2026-10-14 | `DsAccent` is a separate `ThemeExtension` with one constant per theme and accent (`lightSpruce` ... `darkMoss`) and `DsAccent.of(key, brightness)`, an unknown key giving the default; it is not a fourth resolver modifier. `DsColors` keeps its fields. In CSS the light/dark default theme block of an accent also covers an element without `data-theme`, and a `prefers-color-scheme` block covers the other theme, as for the base roles; `data-accent` sits on the same element as `data-theme`. | The tool allows a token to depend on one modifier only, and an accent role always combines with the theme; existing code keeps compiling. |
| D-M4-03-4 | 2026-10-14 | Guard scope: the palette guard "Brand and accent" keeps checking the base roles only; accent variants of an instance are not checked against it. The Discord guard covers every scale. Recorded below the ban list of the design README. | 01 section 12 allows `heather`, `rowan` and `amber` deliberately as the instance owner's choice from the checked palette, while the product brand stays spruce; contrast applies to the variants in full. |
| D-M4-03-5 | 2026-10-14 | An accent without a passing step is reported as a failed gate (exit 1, nothing written, the other gate messages still listed); an invalid `accent_variants` member stays an error of the rules file (exit 2), as for other rule errors. | The brief's test asks for exit 1 when a variant falls below AA; an invalid rules file is a different failure. |

## M7-19 API reference in the docs site

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| M7-19/0 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs of main win, and text this ticket adds is English. Rulings use the row form of this file (`M7-19/<n>` under a ticket heading) instead of the brief's `D-M7-19-<n>` in section 10, as the other ported tickets do. The two `__init__` methods of the hook carry docstrings because main's ruff configuration requires them (rule `D107`). | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| M7-19/1 | 2026-10-14 | Generator (Design note 1): an in-repository MkDocs hook, `tools/docs/mkdocs_api_reference.py`, renders both documents to static Markdown with the pinned `pyyaml` and `mkdocs`; no new dependency. Compared with the pinned Redocly CLI 2.62.1 `build-docs` (plus the AsyncAPI HTML template for the gateway). Evidence: `redocly build-docs docs/api/openapi.yaml` rendered the OpenAPI 3.1 document without errors, but its default page loads `redoc.standalone.js` from `cdn.redocly.com` and a Google Fonts stylesheet, is one 6.9 MB HTML file outside the site's navigation and search, and renders no AsyncAPI; the gateway would need `@asyncapi/cli` and its template as a further npm tree (not in `tools/node`, not tried). The hook renders the 304 operations, 35 webhook events, 560 schemas and 93 gateway messages offline, with no script, in the site's theme, search and strict link and anchor validation; Apache-2.0 as the repository; maintained here. This replaces the suggestion `redocly build-docs` of ADR 0045. | Requirement 9 and 13 (no CDN, no external asset) and requirement 12 (both references in every build); the static hook meets them without bundling a JavaScript application. |
| M7-19/2 | 2026-10-14 | Generated pages live only in the build: `api/reference/rest.md`, `api/reference/schemas.md` and `api/reference/gateway.md` are added as generated files in `on_files` and listed in the nav under API; nothing is committed. A committed file at one of these paths, a missing document, an unresolved local `$ref` or two equal anchors fail the build. Anchors are `op-<operationid lower case>`, `schema-<Name>`, `response-<Name>`, `header-<Name>`, `security-<name>`, `channel-<key>`, `operation-<key>` and `message-<key>` (characters other than letters, digits, `_` and `-` become `-`). Text of the documents is used as Markdown with `<` escaped outside code spans; a code span wrapped over two lines is joined. `docs/api/README.md` names the site paths in code, not as links, because the pages do not exist for `make linkcheck`. | Requirement 12: a stale generated file cannot be committed; the strict build validates every generated link and anchor. |
| M7-19/3 | 2026-10-14 | External asset rule: `tools/docs/external_assets_check.py site` runs in `make docs` after the build and fails on a `<script src>`, a `<link href>` with rel `stylesheet`, `preload`, `modulepreload` or `prefetch`, or a CSS `url(...)` or `@import` (in `<style>`, `style` attributes and `.css` files) whose URL names a host. Anchors and links that load nothing (`rel="canonical"`) pass, `data:` URLs pass, an empty site fails. Requests a bundled script makes at run time are out of its reach; the known one is Material's Mermaid loader (CDN, accepted in ADR 0045 "Consequences"), which the reference pages do not use. | Requirement 13 in the form the brief gives (a check over the built HTML); the run-time loader is an existing, recorded decision and outside this ticket's scope. |
| M7-19/4 | 2026-10-14 | Test first held: `test_external_assets_check.py` and `test_mkdocs_api_reference.py` were written and run red (module not found) before the check and the hook existed. | Honest record of the order of work. |

## M8-01 Discord read client

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M8-01-0 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commit; the English docs of main win. The rows stand in a section per ticket like the other ported tickets. The brief relies on no core rule and no contract change; D-W-51, D-W-100, D-W-120 and D-W-124 are planning rulings in this file. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request (precedent D-M3-17-8 and M1-23/3). |
| D-M8-01-1 | 2026-10-14 | The tool code lives under `services/server/cmd/bothy/importtool/` (D-W-124 (1)); the client is `cmd/bothy/importtool/discord`. `TestDiscordClientImportsNoServerModule` holds that `go list -deps` of the package contains no package under `internal/` except `internal/platform/egress` and `internal/kernel/...`. | `bothy import` is a subcommand of the one binary (component `cmd`), which may use every package; the test keeps the tool on the public API (22 2, R-IM-01). |
| D-M8-01-2 | 2026-10-14 | Rate-limit guard: the route key is the path template plus the guild or channel ID; the bucket is the `X-RateLimit-Bucket` Discord last named for the route, else the path template, again plus that ID. A missing or unparsable wait (`retry_after`, `Retry-After`, and also `X-RateLimit-Reset-After` with `Remaining: 0`) counts as `import.discord.max_rate_wait`; any wait above it ends the call before sleeping with `ErrRateWaitTooLong`, also the wait for an exhausted bucket. After a waited-out `429` the bucket's refill time is cleared, so the retry does not wait twice. The retries after the last allowed `429` end with the new sentinel `ErrRateLimited` (status `429`). `5xx` (`ErrServer`) and transport errors are never retried in the client. | 22 4.3 items 1 to 4: fail towards waiting, never towards hammering (RK-76); the mirror's backoff is M8-05, an import run ends with exit `1` (22 10.3). |
| D-M8-01-3 | 2026-10-14 | `TestClientHasOnlyGet` type-checks the non-test files of the package from source against export data (the approach of `internal/wire/counters_static_test.go`): exactly one call of `http.NewRequest` or `http.NewRequestWithContext`, whose method argument is the object `net/http.MethodGet`; no `http.Request` literal and no use of the field `Method` of a request; no exported name, method or field that matches `post`, `put`, `patch` or `delete` (case-insensitive); no exported function with a parameter named like a method or verb. `TestFakeSeesOnlyGet` drives every exported method against the fake, which answers every other method `405`; `TestNoTestPackageInBinary` lists the dependencies of `cmd/bothy`. | 22 16 "Discord client without a write method", ADR 0043 ruling 1; the literal and field checks close the other ways to send another verb with the one construction. |
| D-M8-01-4 | 2026-10-14 | `MessagesAfter`, `MessagesBefore` and `MessagesAround` return messages in ascending snowflake order, sorted by the numeric value, whatever order Discord used. | Discord returns newest first; the import appends strictly in Discord order (22 6.2 `thread_seq`), so no caller may see another order. |
| D-M8-01-5 | 2026-10-14 | Additions to the names of the brief (D-W-100): `Config.Egress` carries the `egress.*` values of the tool (allowed hosts, timeout, response size limit), because only an operator rule with `allow_private` may open the loopback fake and a consumer policy never can; the host policy of the client names exactly the host and port of `import.discord.api_base` over `https`. The `User-Agent` URL is the constant `https://github.com/OWNER/bothy` (the placeholder of `repo_url` in `mkdocs.yml`), the version comes from `Config.UserAgentVersion`. Further sentinels beside the brief's: `ErrRateLimited`, `ErrUnexpectedStatus` (any other status, a redirect included), `ErrDecode` (a body that is not the expected JSON, without its content), `ErrInvalidID`, `ErrTokenHostMismatch`; `CallError` has a `Hint` (the missing `MANAGE_THREADS`). `Attachment.URL` and `Embed.URL` are of type `SignedURL`, whose print forms show scheme, host and path only and whose `Raw` returns the full link (M8-02). `Deps.Sleep` is a `Sleeper` function. The fixture holds archive pages explicitly; a request beyond the last page gets the last page again. Fixture and test IDs are invented 8-digit strings, because the white-label rule of `tools/denylist/generic.rules` flags 17 to 20 digits as real Discord snowflakes; the client accepts 1 to 20 digits that fit an unsigned 64-bit number. | The brief allows following the code where names differ; each addition keeps a rule of the brief (no proxy, token only to the API base, no secret in output) provable. |
| D-M8-01-6 | 2026-10-14 | `TestTokenOnlyToAPIBase` checks the unexported header setter directly with another host, plain `http` and another port (refused, no header), and a redirect of the fake to a second host name, which the client does not follow (`ErrUnexpectedStatus`, one request, all on the API host). | Every method builds its URL from the API base, so only the setter itself and a redirect can aim a request elsewhere. |

## M7-01 Image bothy-postgres with pgBackRest

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M7-01-0 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win, and text this ticket adds is English. The rows stand in a section per ticket like the other ported tickets. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged names win (D-W-100). |
| D-M7-01-1 | 2026-10-14 | The image lives in `services/server/images/bothy-postgres/` (Dockerfile, `Dockerfile.dockerignore`, `entrypoint.sh`, `check-pgbackrest-version.sh`, README), not in `images/bothy-postgres` of the plan. | The image is an artifact of the component `server` (18 2); there the component path filter of `tools/ci/components.py` sees it. |
| D-M7-01-2 | 2026-10-14 | pgBackRest comes as the package `pgbackrest=2.59.3-1.pgdg13+1` of `apt.postgresql.org` (suite `trixie-pgdg`, the Debian release of the pinned `postgres:18.6-trixie` digest), checked through the repository key of the base image, whose fingerprint is pinned in the Dockerfile. The package stage runs on the build platform, downloads `pgbackrest` and `libssh2-1t64` for the target architecture and unpacks them; the final stage only copies, plus package records under `/var/lib/dpkg/status.d/` for image scanners. `libssh2-1t64` takes the version of the signed Debian archive of that release. The version guard checks the pinned package version in the build and the installed binary on every start. | 18 "Server images" rule 2 forbids emulation; with no step on the target platform one host builds both platforms natively, so `make postgres-image` writes both into one archive. A package exists for both architectures, so no source build is needed (brief design note 1). An exact pin of the Debian library would break the build at every Debian point release, while its integrity is already guaranteed by the signed archive. |
| D-M7-01-3 | 2026-10-14 | `bothy-backupd` is a second `main` package `services/server/cmd/bothy-backupd` with the server version from `internal/platform/buildinfo`; in M7-01 it supervises `pgbackrest server`, creates the stanza `bothy` (retried every `--retry` until it succeeds), stops the child on `SIGTERM` or `SIGINT` (the base image's stop signal), and offers `healthcheck` and `version`. | Brief design note 2: the server binary with its API role has no business in the database image; one commit versions both images. |
| D-M7-01-4 | 2026-10-14 | `pgbackrest_tls/` holds `ca.crt` and per host `postgres` and `backup` a certificate and an ECDSA P-256 PKCS#8 key. The CA is self-signed with path length zero; its key is never written, so a rotation regenerates the directory. Host certificates carry the host name as common name and DNS name and both server and client use, and all three are valid for `stack.PGBackRestTLSValidity`. The whole directory goes to `postgres` and `backup`, as the catalogue row names it. | Both hosts are TLS servers and clients (D-M7-01-5); `tls-server-auth` matches the common name; a stored CA key would let any service holding a copy mint certificates. |
| D-M7-01-5 | 2026-10-14 | Besides PostgreSQL, the container `postgres` runs `pgbackrest server`; the repository host reaches it as `pg1-host` over TLS (`pg1-host-type=tls`) for `stanza-create`, `backup` and `check`. | A repository host can only back up a database whose host it reaches through pgBackRest; without it the full backup "from inside backup" of design note 3 is impossible. It is an inbound connection on the internal network, so R-OP-08 holds. |
| D-M7-01-6 | 2026-10-14 | Both roles run as the user `postgres` of the base image (`USER 999:999`); the entry point never needs root. `secrets-init` gets `--owner postgres=999:999 --owner backup=999:999` (16 5.3 item 4 updated), and the repository and configuration directories belong to that user. The new variable `BOTHY_PGBACKREST_PG_HOST` names the database host for `backup`; `BOTHY_PG_ARCHIVE_TIMEOUT` and both host variables are required. The cipher pass goes only into the configuration file of `backup` (mode `0600`), because pgBackRest reads it from no file of its own. | pgBackRest refuses keys that another user can read (`0444` copies of root failed in the first run); a root image fails the Trivy rule of a non-root user. Host names and timeouts come from named variables (rule 9). |
| D-M7-01-7 | 2026-10-14 | Two CI jobs, `postgres-image (amd64)` on `ubuntu-24.04` and `postgres-image (arm64)` on `ubuntu-24.04-arm`, each with a docker-container buildx builder and no QEMU; they run when the plan lists `server` or `all`, and `ci-ok` (`tools/ci/ci_ok.py`) requires each to end with success or skipped. Written out as two jobs instead of a matrix, because `ci_ok.py` requires the jobs of `ci.yml` by name and a skipped matrix job has no expanded name; the runner rules of `tools/ci/check_runners.py` accept both labels as they are. | B12 and 18 rule 2 need a native arm64 run; the arm64 runner is GitHub-hosted and free for public repositories (D-W-125 rule of no cost). |
| D-M7-01-8 | 2026-10-14 | The two-container test fills the secret volumes of `postgres` and `backup` with a helper container that copies the output of `stack.Init` and sets owner `999:999`, directories `0500` and files `0400`, exactly as `secrets-init` with `--owner` leaves them. The point-in-time target lies at least a second after the end of the full backup. | A test running without root cannot chown files to `999`; pgBackRest selects the backup set by its stop time in whole seconds. |
| D-M7-01-9 | 2026-10-15 | `/etc/pgbackrest` is a link owned by root to `/tmp/bothy-pgbackrest`, which the entry point creates with mode `0700` before it writes `pgbackrest.conf` (and refuses when it is not a directory of the running user); the image copies no configuration directory owned by the user any more. The repository and log directories stay owned by `999:999` (D-M7-01-6). | SonarQube Cloud rates a user-writable copied path below `/etc` as a vulnerability (rule docker:S6504); the default path `/etc/pgbackrest/pgbackrest.conf` stays, so pgBackRest, `docker compose exec` and the docs need no option. |

## M8-02 CDN fetch with host allow list

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M8-02-0 | 2026-10-15 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the rows stand in a section per ticket like the other ported tickets. Brief, spec and REUSE were read from `main`; the brief relies on no core rule and no contract change, and chapter 22 already describes the behaviour (6.5, 14, 16), so no spec text changes. Additions to the names of the brief (D-W-100): `CDNConfig` also carries `MaxRetries`, `MaxRateWait` (the `429` limits of requirement 16), `UserAgentVersion` and `Egress` (as in `Config` of M8-01, so that only a test rule with `allow_private` opens the loopback fake); `AttachmentRef.URL` is a `SignedURL`, so that a printed reference never shows the signature; the one request construction of the package moved into `newGet`, which the API client and the fetcher share, because `TestClientHasOnlyGet` allows exactly one; the fetcher also returns `*CallError` (op `GET <scheme://host/path>`) and the `429` sentinels of the client. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases and main takes each ticket as one pull request (precedent D-M8-01-0); the brief allows following the code where names differ, and each addition keeps a rule of the brief provable. |
| D-M8-02-1 | 2026-10-15 | One `platform/egress` client per fetcher with a host policy of exactly the entries of `import.discord.cdn_hosts` over `https`, `RedirectNever`, `Timeout` = `import.http_timeout` and `MaxResponseBytes` = `import.attachment_max_bytes` plus one, instead of one transport per allowed host (REUSE 5.2). The fetcher checks every link against the same list before the request, so `egress.allowed_hosts` adds no CDN host. | The per-request host policy gives the same guarantee: no connection is shared across a host the policy did not allow, because the pool keys connections by scheme, host and port. |
| D-M8-02-2 | 2026-10-15 | Re-read rule: on `403` or `404` the body is closed unread, `MessagesAround(channel, message, 1)` reads the message once, the attachment with the same ID (also in a forwarded message snapshot) gives the fresh link, which passes the URL check and the declared size check again, and is fetched once. A missing message or attachment, or a second `403` or `404`, is `ErrCDNGone`; `5xx` and other statuses are a `*CallError` without retry. A `429` waits like the read client (`retry_after`, else `Retry-After`; a missing value counts as `import.discord.max_rate_wait`, a longer wait ends with `ErrRateWaitTooLong`); its body is read only for that value. | 22 6.5 item 3 asks for exactly one re-read and one more try; the caller writes the jump-link line for a failed attachment (22 6.5 item 6, M8-04). |
| D-M8-02-3 | 2026-10-15 | Port rule: a link matches an entry when its lower-cased host without one trailing dot equals the entry and its port equals the entry's port, `443` when the entry names none; an explicit `:443` matches an entry without a port. Entries are DNS names with an optional `:port`, never IP literals or wildcards. | Production entries name no port; tests write the fake's port into the entry as `host:port` (Design note 1). |

## M6-10 Color vision deficiency check for charts

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M6-10-0 | 2026-10-15 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commit; the chart roles `color.viz.*`, their contrast pairing and the preview `viz` of plan ruling D-W-13 are on main already, so the base's port of them is left out. The rows stand in a section per ticket like the other ported tickets. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request (D-W-100). |
| D-M6-10-1 | 2026-10-15 | Simulation model: the matrices of Machado, Oliveira and Fernandes 2009 (IEEE TVCG 15(6), doi:10.1109/TVCG.2009.113) for severity 1.0, for protanopia, deuteranopia and tritanopia, in `tokenkit/cvd.py` with the citation. They are applied in linear sRGB: decode, multiply, clip to [0, 1], encode. Tests pin every column against the published numbers, white and greys unchanged, and two samples worked by hand from the table. | One model for all three deficiencies, in linear RGB, as browser developer tools use it; the gate needs a reproducible transformation, not a perceptual study (brief, design note 1). |
| D-M6-10-2 | 2026-10-15 | Distance: OKLab Euclidean distance of the two simulated colours (`delta_e_ok`), computed on the unrounded simulated values. `cvd_min_delta_e_ok` is 0.05 in `design/design-rules.json`, with source and reason there: 2.5 times the just noticeable difference 0.02 of deltaE OK (CSS Color 4), because chart marks are small and the noticeable difference grows as marks shrink (Szafir 2018). A row passes when the distance rounded to three places reaches the minimum, as contrast rows round to two. Measured minimum before and after the adjustment (series; traffic light): light protanopia 0.056 to 0.071; 0.069 to 0.123, deuteranopia 0.012 to 0.094; 0.008 to 0.083, tritanopia 0.076 to 0.076; 0.095 to 0.079; dark protanopia 0.054 to 0.086; 0.071 to 0.075, deuteranopia 0.012 to 0.067; 0.007 to 0.059, tritanopia 0.116 to 0.116; 0.011 to 0.099. | The measure the palette guards already use; the factor is a stated design choice, not a measured value (brief, design note 2). |
| D-M6-10-3 | 2026-10-15 | Changed values (alias steps of the same scale, so hue and series order stay): light `color.viz.series.3` `spruce.600` (`#1b8a83`) to `spruce.700` (`#136f69`), `color.viz.series.6` `moss.600` (`#1b9046`) to `moss.900` (`#063e1a`), `color.warning.fg` `amber.700` (`#8c4f10`) to `amber.800` (`#6b3b09`), `color.danger.fg` `rowan.700` (`#b21622`) to `rowan.900` (`#63080e`); dark `color.viz.series.3` `spruce.400` (`#49c0b7`) to `spruce.300` (`#7cd5cd`), `color.viz.series.7` `rowan.400` (`#fb827a`) to `rowan.500` (`#f15653`), `color.danger.fg` `rowan.300` (`#fcaaa3`) to `rowan.400` (`#fb827a`). Two series per theme is the fewest that passes (exhaustive search over the steps of each series' own scale); no palette step changes, so no other pairing moves. The traffic light failed under deuteranopia in both themes and under tritanopia in dark; the state roles changed because every contrast pairing of banners, badges and live box stays green in both themes and with every accent (`make tokens`). All previews are regenerated, because the state roles appear in the components, layout, mobile and palette sheets too. | Brief, design note 4: fewest series, lightness before hue, guards and contrast green; the state roles only with the banner and badge pairings green. |
| D-M6-10-4 | 2026-10-15 | Pairs (`cvd_pairs`): a `sequence` entry with the eight series and `wrap: true` gives 1 with 2 up to 7 with 8 and 8 with 1; a `pairs` entry gives `success.fg` with `warning.fg`, `warning.fg` with `danger.fg` and `success.fg` with `danger.fg`; all under the three deficiencies, per theme (`light`, `dark`). The accents of M4-03 swap none of these roles, so there are no per-accent CVD rows; a test fails if an accent role joins the checked roles. Rows go to `contrast.md` (section "Color vision deficiency") and to the member `cvd` of `tokens.resolved.json`. | Brief, design notes 3 and 5. |

## M4-01 Dart workspace and architecture test

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M4-01-0 | 2026-10-14 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win (chapters `15-client-architecture.md` and `23-quality.md`), text this ticket adds is English, and its first commit (R-QA-14 into chapter 23) is left out because main has R-QA-14 since its baseline. `ci-ok` on main has no `needs` and requires the jobs of `ci.yml` by name through `RULES` in `tools/ci/ci_ok.py`, so the two app jobs are added there. | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged names win (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M4-01-1 | 2026-10-14 | The Flutter pin lives in `environment.flutter` of the root `pubspec.yaml` (`3.47.7`, Dart `^3.13.5`), CI reads it there (`flutter-version-file: pubspec.yaml` in `gates`, `sonar`, `apps-macos`, `apps-windows` and the Pages build), and every member repeats the same exact `environment` lines; `test_flutter_pins_agree` holds the fifteen manifests equal. Replaces D-M2-38-12. | One known Flutter for every job and member; the workspace root is the one manifest every member shares. |
| D-M4-01-2 | 2026-10-14 | `packages/bothy_noise` joins the workspace (`resolution: workspace`), its `pubspec.lock` is deleted and its code and tests stay unchanged; its OSV-Scanner overrides move with the lock file to the root `osv-scanner.toml`, with a new checked entry for every package or version the one resolution adds. The dev dependency `test` is `^1.31.1` in every pure Dart member, because `flutter_test` of Flutter 3.47.7 pins `test_api` 0.7.12 and `test` 1.33 needs 0.7.15; one resolution cannot hold both. Replaces D-M2-38-13; D-M2-38-20 applies to the root file. | Brief note 1; OSV-Scanner reads the override file next to the lock file it scans. |
| D-M4-01-3 | 2026-10-14 | The reach of the diagram of 15 3.1 is an allow list: `tools/arch-check/rules.yaml` holds the arrows, and a member may depend on and import exactly the members they reach (arrows and transitive closure), checked in `make arch`. A new arrow changes 15 3.1 and `rules.yaml` in the same change; `test_rules_edges_equal_the_spec_diagram` holds both equal. 15 3.1 gains rule 8 (the reach) and rule 9 (every manifest a listed member), and the check details of rules 1, 4 and 7. Findings on a manifest name its line 1. | A deny list would let every new edge in silently (brief note 2). |
| D-M4-01-4 | 2026-10-14 | Generated Dart code (`*.g.dart`, `*.freezed.dart`, `*.drift.dart`, `lib/src/generated/`, `build/`) is out of the doc lint and the style lint (analyzer `exclude` in the root `analysis_options.yaml`), the format check (`DART_GENERATED` in the root `Makefile`) and coverage (`tools/ci/lcov_filter.py`). It stays in the compile check: the members that import it are analyzed, and `flutter test` compiles it. An excluded file is not analyzed itself, so an analyzer error inside it shows only through its users and the compiler. Each member includes its base set and then the root file through a list under `include` (Dart 3.6 and later). | Generators format and document their own output (M4-02, M4-12); the same split as M2-23 note 8 for the other languages. |
| D-M4-01-5 | 2026-10-14 | `make lint` runs `flutter pub get --enforce-lockfile` and `flutter analyze --fatal-infos` once at the workspace root, which analyzes every member, and `dart run custom_lint` in each member that declares it; `make test` resolves once at the root and runs `flutter test --coverage` per member, after checking that every member has `test/`. | One resolution and one analysis for the workspace (brief note 4); a stale lock file still fails instead of being rewritten (D-M2-38-21). |
| D-M4-01-6 | 2026-10-14 | The Dart API pages are generated for every build and never tracked: `tools/docs/dart_api_docs.py` runs `dart doc` per package into the ignored folder `docs/api/dart/`, and a warning or an error fails it. `make docs` runs it whenever a Dart member is tracked, for every component selection, not only with `apps`, because `docs/reference/dart-api.md` links the pages and `mkdocs build --strict` fails on a missing target; `make linkcheck` builds them first when the folder is missing, for the same reason (lychee checks the links offline). No validation setting changes. | Generated pages cannot drift from the code (brief note 5); the strict site build and the link check stay as they are. |
| D-M4-01-7 | 2026-10-14 | Platform tests are outside `gates`: `make apps-platform` (`tools/ci/apps_platform.py`) runs the argument lists of each member's `platform-tests.json` for the host system, and the CI jobs `apps (macOS)` (`macos-15`) and `apps (Windows)` (`windows-2025`) run it when the `apps` component is selected; `ci-ok` requires each to end with `success` or `skipped`. | They need a desktop session (brief note 6, ADR 0039); a skipped job (component not selected) must not block. |
| D-M4-01-8 | 2026-10-14 | The root files `pubspec.yaml`, `pubspec.lock` and `analysis_options.yaml` and the prefix `tools/arch-check/` select the gate component `apps` (`tools/ci/components.py`). | A change there must run the Dart gates (brief note 7); before, the root files selected `all`. |
| D-M4-01-9 | 2026-10-14 | The packages of the Flutter SDK in the root lock file (`flutter`, `flutter_test`, `sky_engine`; source `sdk`, version 0.0.0) get license overrides in `osv-scanner.toml`: BSD-3-Clause, the license of the Flutter repository. The LICENSE file of `sky_engine` also collects the notices of the third-party code compiled into the engine (Apache-2.0, MIT, BSD, Zlib, Unicode, IJG and FreeType texts among them); its review is the ScanCode step before a release (docs/quality/licenses.md item 4). | deps.dev does not know SDK packages, so the license gate reports them as UNKNOWN; every Flutter app ships the engine (ADR 0027). |
| D-M4-01-10 | 2026-10-14 | Skeletons: each package library is a `library;` directive with its doc comment and no other public symbol, so each first test imports it with `// ignore: unused_import` (the import is what it checks). `device_key` is created from `flutter create --template=plugin --platforms=macos,windows` reduced to a Flutter package without native files, without the example app and without a `flutter: plugin:` section, because no platform class exists until M4-10. The apps show an empty `MaterialApp` without text and have no platform folders yet. | Brief note 8 and rule 4: no native code before its ticket. |
| D-M4-01-11 | 2026-10-14 | `packages/api_client` gets a `Makefile` whose `generate-check` passes while no generated code is tracked and fails as soon as `lib/src/generated/` or a `*.g.dart` file is tracked; M4-02 replaces the target with the generation, the compile and the comparison. `make api-clients` stays fail-closed for a manifest without the target. `tools/ci/apps_platform.py` resolves the program of each command with `shutil.which` before it runs it. | Code review M4-01: the tracked skeleton manifest made `make api-clients` (and so `make gates` and CI) fail, and without a shell Windows does not find `flutter.bat` for the job `apps-windows`. |
| D-M4-01-12 | 2026-10-15 | Port onto main: `dart doc` links Google Fonts (a preconnect and two stylesheets) in every page, which the external asset check of M7-19 refuses, so `tools/docs/dart_api_docs.py` removes every `<link>` to another origin from the generated pages, which then use the fonts of the system. The generated folder `api/dart/` is listed in `not_in_nav` of `mkdocs.yml`, because main's strict build fails on a page missing from the navigation and `docs/reference/dart-api.md` is the entry to those pages. | The site loads nothing from another origin (M7-19/3); the generated pages are reached through one reference page, like the single ADRs through the ADR index. |

## M7-10 Server release chain with a trial release

| No | Date | Ruling | Reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-M7-10-0 | 2026-10-15 | The ticket was built on an integration base and ported onto main with only its own commits; the English docs and spec of main win. The runbook keeps main's layout: the server steps stand in `release.md` 2.1, the trial release in 2.3, the key pair of the private phase in 2.4, the verification commands in 5. On main the release workflow follows the stricter rules of `tools/ci/check_runners.py` (release jobs resolve the tag again, outputs of jobs without a release environment only in `if:`, artifacts only by id), so `postgres-build-amd64`, `postgres-build-arm64` and `scancode` run in the environment `release` and resolve the tag themselves (D-M7-10-8). | The tickets are built in parallel on integration bases; main takes each ticket as one pull request; merged rules win (D-W-100, D-W-114). |
| D-M7-10-1 | 2026-10-15 | Trial release (Design note 1): `make release-trial COMPONENT=server` runs `tools/ci/release_trial.py`, which starts `registry:3.1.2` pinned by digest on `127.0.0.1` with a random port, creates a `docker-container` buildx builder in the host network (so BuildKit reaches the registry on `127.0.0.1`), runs `make -C services/server release-build`, pushes `bothy-server` (both platforms, one build) and `bothy-postgres` (one build per platform) by digest, joins the postgres digests, generates a cosign key pair in the temporary directory, and then calls the same `tools/ci/release_chain.py` functions as `release.yml` (`sbom`, `sign --mode private`, `verify --mode private`, `tag`). It checks every image `infra/compose/compose.yaml` names with `${BOTHY_VERSION}` against the signed digest and runs three probes that must fail (changed file against `SHA256SUMS`, unsigned child digest against `cosign verify`, a release without one SBOM). cosign and syft get an empty `DOCKER_CONFIG`, so no credential of the host enters. `TRIAL_PLATFORMS` defaults to both platforms for both images, unlike the brief's default of the host platform for `bothy-postgres`: the merged Dockerfile of M7-01 builds the foreign platform of `bothy-postgres` without emulation (D-M7-01-2). Vulnerability scan and ScanCode are not part of the trial; they are the separate targets `make trivy-image` and `make scancode`. The trial and the target refuse to run when `GITHUB_ACTIONS` is set. | D-W-127; the same code in trial and release is what makes the trial a proof; the merged code wins over the brief's default (D-W-100); a scan result depends on the vulnerability database of the day, not on the chain. |

Verification `cosign verify --key <pub> --insecure-ignore-tlog <ref@digest>`, `cosign verify-blob --key <pub> --insecure-ignore-tlog --bundle <bundle> <file>`, `sha256sum --check --strict SHA256SUMS`. Public phase: the same commands without `--key` and signing configuration (keyless with the OIDC token of the job), verified with `--certificate-identity-regexp '^https://github.com/<owner>/bothy/.github/workflows/release.yml@refs/tags/server/v' --certificate-oidc-issuer https://token.actions.githubusercontent.com`, plus `actions/attest` per image digest; the mode follows `github.event.repository.visibility`. `SHA256SUMS` covers every file of the release except the signature bundles. Calibration: a run of ScanCode 32.5.0 over the tracked tree at this branch found 38 files outside the list, all text that names licenses or reviewed data (documentation, tool manifests and lock files, the OFL-1.1 fonts of docs/design/typography.md rule 5 in `design/` and `tools/design-tokens/`, the Unlicense Noise vectors of D-M2-38-15, the OSV-Scanner overrides, the SPDX header templates of the server's code generators, DCO and CLA in `CONTRIBUTING.md`); `scancode-exceptions.txt` lists these paths with their reasons, and the same report then has no finding. That run used the PyPI package `scancode-toolkit-mini` 32.5.0 natively, because the container was killed twice for memory in the shared Docker VM of the build host; the release job runs the container on an amd64 runner. | licenses.md section 4; a digest-pinned base plus a checksum-pinned archive fixes the image as rule 9 of the brief asks; a gate needs a decision rule, and documentation that lists forbidden licenses must not stop a release. |
